# Full SDK Support Work

## Current accepted status (2026-10-07)

**The pinned SDK integration is complete within the user's agreed scope.**
[sdk-acceptance.md](sdk-acceptance.md) defines the current requirements, generic
desktop mapping/xterm boundaries and verification limits. The complete SDK remains
available in Node; APIs do not all require dedicated UI controls. No outstanding
in-scope functional gap was found in the bounded final audit.

All eleven InteractiveModeOptions fields are supported, including renderer mode,
initial theme, a live custom Terminal and native implicit trust after reload.
See [invocation-options.md](invocation-options.md). The category's logged passing
union is **140 source cases / 38 browser scenarios**, with **four native groups**,
passing type/export checks, one executable build and final backend restaging.
Five final desktop/narrow screenshots were inspected. Failed runs and affected
reruns remain explicit in `.local/invocation-receipt.json`; no fresh green full
source/default browser suite or rebuilt final installer is claimed.

Arbitrary private component inference, exhaustive async combinations, all devices
and services, exact terminal palette/geometry and unknown future versions are
compatibility boundaries, not additional completion blockers. Physical OS IME,
manual system clipboard readback and real-account service validation retain their
stated evidence limits.

## Historical category records

The records below describe their state at the time. Earlier open/completion
statements are superseded by the current status and acceptance contract; original
failures, repairs and environment limitations remain recorded.

## Background startup policies (2026-10-07)

The desktop now runs Pi's original catalog-refresh coordinator, version checker, package-update checker, tmux keyboard diagnostics and subscription-auth warnings. DesktopSdkContext.startupPolicies and the startup-policy.inspect/run actions make the same behavior usable without a new UI control. Native startup gates, timeout, independent catalog cancellation, original package display names, scoped footer counts and one-time warning flags are retained. Raw SDK calls preserve native result objects; JSON transport converts catalog error Maps to provider diagnostics. Original notice components use the generic desktop mapper. See [the policy contract](startup-policies.md).

Original crash-log utilities retain session/cwd/stack, record age, pending announcement, retention and clearing. Assistant errors use the native retry/cancellation filters and /bug hint. Real uncaught crashes revealed a supervisor lifetime bug: recording and worker exit succeeded while the HTTP host remained open. Fatal exit now flushes an explicit native-channel shutdown event, then exits; the supervisor drains events and waits for the worker. An intermediate fix closed every terminal exit and failed two existing history/lifetime cases; the final implementation preserves ordinary terminal inspection.

The initial full source group passes **1198/1214**, with 14 failures and two cancellations. Consolidated fixture/type fixes pass **139/140** affected cases and **46/46** browser scenarios, leaving the confirmed crash bug. Expanded lifetime/crash coverage initially passes **138/142**, exposing the intermediate exit behavior, abrupt direct-crash SSE observation and tmux teardown. The subsequent group passes **54/55**: only tmux fixture cleanup remains. Its timeout stub also stalled native synchronous hyperlink probing, leaving a Windows shell-launched copied executable that blocked removal. Keyboard timeout and display-message behavior are now distinct and owned stubs have bounded lifetimes. The complete final policy source group passes **33/33**. Across the stated logs, **142 distinct affected source cases** and **1215 distinct source cases** have passing evidence; no fresh final fully green complete source suite is claimed.

The related browser group passes **46/46**, including desktop/narrow policy, startup, application-content and continuity workflows. The final policy/presentation group passes **10/10**, overlapping that coverage. Screenshots collapse test-process raw color warnings to show the original desktop notices. The initial package, ordinary transport/PTY and browser-reload timeouts pass the bounded affected groups, but their original causes remain unestablished; no product correction is claimed for those failures. Final types/formatting and the unchanged **458-declaration / 156-native-export** SDK audit pass.

One type-checked unbundled build passes, followed by **three native groups / nineteen workflows** against that executable: startup-policy (five), startup (four), runtime-continuity (ten). All **three staged crash workflows** pass with the packaged Node/backend: direct SDK, HTTP PTY and native-channel PTY. Backend hashes agree across all three staged copies. Exact run/repair boundaries, source/artifact/log/screenshot hashes and preview ownership are in .local/startup-policy-receipt.json. The preview keeps **http://127.0.0.1:1451/**. Its previous owned backend/Vite processes were absent; replacement follows free-port checks and records new process/backend/session identities. The previous exit cause and original tab connection remain unverified. No tab is added, installer rebuilt or full default browser run claimed.

**Full SDK completion remains open.** Remaining layout/theme/custom-terminal invocation options and the final requirement audit are separate targets. Private ownership/geometry, broader terminal protocols, physical devices/external accounts, installed-copy behavior and future SDK upgrades retain their separate verification limits. Older sections below preserve historical evidence.

## Startup content and initial inputs (2026-10-07)

The desktop now invokes Pi's original startup/full changelog methods, preserves version filtering and collapsed/expanded native components, and exposes sequential initial messages/images plus diagnostics, migration and model-fallback notices. Verbose launch configuration reaches the original header receiver. Constructor, initialization, DesktopSdkContext.startup and startup.inspect/run/changelog provide access without requiring a new UI control. Same-workspace reconnection does not replay messages. See [the startup contract](startup.md).

The active initial-input batch observes its original session's public abort boundary. Accepted new/switch/fork/import transitions and direct abort stop it before asynchronous native shutdown handlers finish, while preserving those handlers' current session/extension lifetime. Cancelled transitions and validation failures retain the batch. Native prompt argument lists, error continuation, original abort Promises/receivers/errors and owned descriptor restoration remain intact. SDK sources, exports and prototypes are unchanged. Seven isolated workers verify native install-telemetry gates and initial-input differential behavior, capturing requests locally; normal fixtures disable telemetry.

Whole-category validation initially passes **1168/1174** source cases and **28/28** related browser scenarios. Old header-version expectations, synchronous HTTP-close observations and a UI reload entry point account for the six first-run failures. The first affected repair passes **70/71**, with one timeout: a newly added real session-transition case reveals that the initial batch can start another prompt before native invalidation, wedging cancellation. The verified owned hung test worker is stopped so its original harness records the timeout. The product repair then passes **103/104** affected cases; its remaining failure is a fixture assumption that a missing session file is invalid, although native Pi creates it. That run also exposes post-test timeout-cleanup listeners. After consolidated fixture repairs, the complete startup group passes **30/30**, including all transition variants, cancelled/invalid transitions and delayed shutdown. Together the logs establish **104 distinct passing affected cases** and **1182 distinct passing source cases**; no fresh final fully green complete source run is claimed. The final related browser batch passes **28/28** again. Type checking and the unchanged **458-declaration / 156-native-export** audit pass.

One type-checked unbundled native build passes, followed by **three native groups / twelve workflows** against the same executable: startup (four), application-content (four) and managed-tools (four). No installer or full default browser run is claimed. Exact failed-run/repair boundaries, source/artifact/log/screenshot hashes and preview ownership are recorded in .local/startup-receipt.json. The retained **http://127.0.0.1:1451/** preview reuses Vite and refreshes its owned source backend after identity/idle checks; no browser tab is added, and the original tab connection remains unverified.

**Full SDK completion remains open.** Background catalog/version/package/tmux/crash/subscription policies and remaining layout/theme/terminal invocation integration are separate targets. Private ownership/geometry, broader terminal protocols, physical-device/external-account/installed-copy behavior and future SDK upgrades retain their separate verification limits. Older entries below preserve historical evidence.

## Managed tools and original file completion (2026-10-07)

The desktop now calls Pi's unchanged fd/rg tool manager before initial editor binding and supplies its fd result to the original CombinedAutocompleteProvider. Recursive and scoped @file completion, candidate ordering and application use the native engine. Host-owned preparation joins concurrent callers, retains original unavailable results, supports explicit retries and survives session/workspace changes. Inspection, native path lookup and preparation are available through DesktopSdkContext.managedTools and managed-tools.inspect/path/prepare, including before workspace initialization. Original managed-status Spacer/ThemedText instances enter the shared application tree in callback order and map generically to desktop controls. See [the managed-tool contract](managed-tools.md).

Explicit preparation rebuilds the base completion provider while retaining extension wrapper factories in original order and merging their trigger characters. Registration follows original InteractiveMode rebuilding semantics; resource/session reset clears wrappers before extension startup. Waiting consumers settle promptly on retirement, and late callbacks/paths cannot enter the retired host. The original downloader exposes no cancellation API, so its bounded download/cache operation may finish after retirement; native network cancellation is not claimed. Pi source, exports and prototypes remain unchanged.

Following the user's category-level workflow, the initial complete source batch passes **1152/1152**, type checking and the unchanged **458-declaration / 156-native-export** audit pass, and the related browser batch passes **34/40**. Six new completion workflows incorrectly pressed Tab after Pi had already opened its suggestion list, thereby accepting the first candidate. The consolidated repair removes that extra keypress and fixes loss of extension wrappers on explicit preparation, with a new differential source regression and desktop/narrow custom-wrapper workflows. The final affected source group passes **61/61** and browser group **12/12**, establishing **1153 distinct passing source cases** and **40 distinct passing affected browser scenarios** across the stated runs. No fresh final completely green full source or full default browser suite is claimed. Five isolated integration workers exercise unchanged native preinstalled/offline/download/failure-retry/retirement paths with real binaries and local HTTP archives; offline startup status buffering and completion after extraction are included.

One final type-checked unbundled native build passes. The first managed-tools native attempt hits a five-second preinitialization assertion timeout before any SDK action; its test-only repair waits for application readiness, as the existing native workflows do. The other two initial native groups pass, and the complete managed-tools rerun passes against the unchanged executable, establishing **three native groups / fifteen workflows**: managed-tools (four), default-editor (one), runtime-continuity (ten). No product startup fix is claimed. The installer was not rebuilt. Exact source/artifact/log/screenshot hashes and preview ownership are in .local/managed-tools-receipt.json. The existing **http://127.0.0.1:1451/** preview reuses Vite and refreshes its owned source backend after identity/idle checks. No browser tab is added; the original tab connection remains unverified.

**Full SDK completion remains open.** Remaining CLI changelog/initial-message/startup policies and prior private-geometry/terminal-protocol, physical-device/external-account/installed-copy and future-upgrade limits remain separate targets. Older entries below preserve historical evidence.

## Native application content (2026-10-07)

The shared application tree now uses Pi's original builtin header, resource sections and diagnostics, pending queue rows and string-widget containers. An isolated original InteractiveMode receiver constructs them without changing Pi source, exports or prototypes. The private builtin header is captured at the original completed-header init boundary, before managed-tool setup or session binding. Original quiet modes, configured hints, expansion, theme regeneration, resource path/scope/package grouping, pending truncation/dequeue hint and the ten-line widget limit remain authoritative. See [the content contract](application-content.md). Full CLI startup, changelog and managed-tool policies remain separate targets.

Mixed string/factory widgets retain explicit Map order, including numeric keys, placement and repeated shared native occurrences. Desktop string presentation renders the original Container through the generic ANSI mapper. Factory components retain existing registry ownership and disposal. Ordinary session/resource resets preserve the builtin header and shared application owner; hard retirement clears all content even if one cleanup throws. Normal desktop conversation/queue/resource views retain React presentation; the same native objects remain accessible through SDK operations and map through generic public header/widget factories without per-extension adapters. Opaque components and raw programs continue to use xterm.js.

Whole-category verification initially passes **1124/1127** source cases and **42/44** related browser scenarios. The consolidated product/fixture repair full source run passes **1134/1135**; after final test-only padding/locator changes the affected source group passes **101/101**. These logs establish **1135 distinct passing source cases**, without claiming a fresh final fully green complete suite. Expanded browser coverage passes **17/19**, then the final six-case style group passes **6/6**, establishing **55 distinct passing affected browser scenarios** with overlap accounted for. Type checking, code/document formatting, the unchanged SDK audit and one type-checked unbundled native build pass. All **five native groups / eighteen workflows** pass against that executable: application-content, runtime-continuity, renderer-modes, terminal-effects and terminal. Exact failed-run boundaries, source/artifact/log/screenshot hashes and preview ownership are in `.local/application-content-receipt.json`. The installer and complete default browser suite were not rebuilt/rerun.

Old expectations assumed a simplified extension heading, unpadded strings and fixed ANSI-run boundaries. The pending probe incorrectly called setText on original TruncatedText, which exposes a text field instead. These observations now follow original components. Full source verification also exposes a real Windows startup-title recognition failure: ConPTY can insert cursor CSI sequences between its clear/reset and initial executable title. The recognizer now accepts a CSI-only initialization preamble across chunks while preserving explicit titles, printable extension output, unrelated OSC and replay ordering. Eight source regressions and the real supervisor, browser xterm and native terminal-effect workflows cover the correction. Final test-only fixes retain Pi's moved-widget leading space and locate the actual merged colored run.

The retained **http://127.0.0.1:1451/** preview reuses the existing Vite process and refreshes its owned source backend after checking identity and idle state. No new tab is added; the original tab connection remains unverified. Current process/backend/session identities are recorded in the receipt.

**Full SDK completion remains open.** Remaining CLI startup/policy behavior, arbitrary private ownership/geometry, broader terminal protocols, physical-device/external-account/installed-copy behavior and future-version compatibility retain their separate completion limits. Older paragraphs below preserve their historical evidence and boundaries.

## Native conversation notices (2026-10-07)

Cache warming, significant cache misses, compaction/branch-summary billing and live dropped-thinking notices now call Pi's original InteractiveMode methods and original cache scanner. The same original Spacer/ThemedText objects populate the shared native chat tree and generic ANSI-mapped desktop conversation rows. Original thresholds, cost/token formatting, label precedence, settings gates and previous-branch diagnostics remain authoritative. Live message-end detection runs before persistence and adopts its existing native instances afterwards. Repeated warming events do not duplicate rows, context omission removes hidden message diagnostics, and direct native changes/theme invalidation retain original semantics. Live compaction follows branch chronology; resume/settings/resource rebuild uses native context-entry reconstruction order. Settings/reload/session transitions clear transient thinking notices and re-derive persisted usage/cache costs. SDK messages and JSONL remain unchanged by presentation.

See [the notice contract](conversation-notices.md).

The complete product source suite passes **1107/1107**. After final test-only namespace-import/locator repairs, the complete notice category passes **29/29** source cases and **6/6** browser workflows. Source/artifact/log/screenshot hashes and exact run boundaries are in `.local/conversation-notices-receipt.json`. The installer and complete default browser suite were not rebuilt/rerun.

The first complete source run passes 1100/1104 and the first browser group passes 36/42. Formatting also separated a multiline untyped fixture import from its expect-error diagnostic; a stable namespace import fixes type checking. These are fixture/observation repairs; no product correction is claimed for them.

## Resource reload and session continuity (2026-10-07)

Ordinary resource reload and SDK new/switch/fork/import transitions now retain one workspace interactive runtime: Pi's original stable TUI reference, terminal, application containers, default CustomEditor/history, FooterComponent/provider, direct registered children and direct input/debug/color registrations. Extension-context input subscriptions and session surfaces are cleared, and captured extension pi/ctx objects retain the SDK's original stale-context checks. Complete old tool rows retire; a new Bash row owner prevents late old execution chunks from entering the successor session. Settings and the original keybindings manager reload on the retained editor; footer/provider bind the current session. Workspace replacement, explicit clearSurfaces and shutdown still retire the entire runtime. See [the lifecycle contract](runtime-lifecycle.md).

Top-overlay hiding and default-editor focus restoration have differential coverage using original InteractiveMode resetExtensionUI/setCustomEditorComponent. Direct TUI and context unsubscribe behavior is compared with the original implementation, including repeated unsubscribe after identical-handler re-registration. Lower direct overlays and the global registration mapping endpoint retain their original ownership; stale asynchronous factory results cannot mount again. Standard controls remain desktop components; opaque components retain real xterm input, original mouse focus requests and existing state without per-extension adaptation.

The initial full source batch passes **1055/1064**; it predates consolidated observation/type/fixture repairs and an additional direct-unsubscribe regression. Its nine failures comprise an old color-lifetime expectation, five tests reading the deliberately hidden empty registration surface, a padding expectation outside Pi's supported range, a differential receiver that omitted original default-editor focus restoration, and a Windows OSC assertion requiring a cursor-clear/title adjacency that ConPTY does not guarantee. The repair affected group passes **229/231**; its two failures call a nonexistent native helper in new differential tests. After changing only those calls to the actual handleTerminalInput method, the overlapping final lifecycle/input group passes **27/27**. Together these logs establish passing evidence for all **231 distinct affected source cases**; no fresh final full source suite is claimed. Logs are .local/runtime-continuity-source-{group1,repair,final}.log.

The grouped browser batch initially passes **49/60**. Ten opaque scenarios omit an original mouse focus handler after the default editor regains focus; one footer scenario assumes the provider probe count resets across sessions, although the provider now correctly persists. With the fixture requesting focus through its original handler and the count measured relative to its retained baseline, the complete affected browser group passes **60/60** at 1440px/390px in .local/runtime-continuity-ui-repair.log. Its 20 new scenarios cover reload/new/switch/fork/import with both mapped Input and actual xterm, persistent instance/value/history/direct hooks/color notifications, stale SDK contexts and exactly-once removal. Product behavior is unchanged by those fixture repairs. Type checking passes; the type-checked unbundled build updates all three backend copies and the executable. SDK audit retains **458 declaration exports, 156 unchanged native runtime exports, 38 constructors and 16 declaration static members (15 at runtime)**.

All **seven native groups** pass against that one executable without further product repairs: runtime-continuity (ten mapped/xterm workflows across reload/new/switch/fork/import), default-editor, runtime-callbacks, terminal-state, overlay-lifecycle, native-execution and terminal. Logs and source/artifact/screenshot hashes are recorded in `.local/runtime-continuity-receipt.json`. Representative wide, narrow and native screenshots were inspected.

The existing **http://127.0.0.1:1451/** preview retains Vite PID 26936 and uses refreshed source backend PID 24920 on 4351. Backend `a791dc9f-d24b-44df-a8a0-7a1e7701ee4a` and session `01a11302-9859-7231-970d-118f9d203259` have initialization/snapshot evidence. The previous backend's ownership and idle state were checked before graceful replacement. No new browser tab was added; the available browser automation inventory reports no tabs, so the original tab connection remains unverified.

Renderer-mode replacement (regular/fullscreen), exact native redraw/force accounting, full terminal start/stop lifecycle, broader specialized notices and complete original application-tree coverage remain open. Broader terminal protocols/private ownership/geometry and physical-device/external-account/installed-copy/future-upgrade verification retain their separate limits. The installer and full default browser suite were not rebuilt/rerun. **Full SDK completion remains open.** Older reload/reference boundaries below are superseded only for the ordinary workspace lifecycle described here.

## Complete original tool rows, live bash and host notices (2026-10-07)

The shared application tree now contains one complete original ToolExecutionComponent per toolCallId, original live BashExecutionComponent rows and original Spacer/ThemedText host notices. Tool rows own call/result callback state, lastComponent, streamed phases, fallback, image and expansion behavior. The native tree contains each complete row once; desktop phase surfaces reuse its callback children and original result mouse policy. Only generic desktop fallback text projects images into the separate DOM image region; full native rows and SDK results retain their original image content and terminal behavior. Custom callback components remain the original objects.

Session-instance observers preserve public executeBash/recordBashResult options, receivers, chunk callbacks, return values and errors, without modifying SDK exports or prototypes. Concurrent calls have independent original live rows. Streaming-time bash appears in pending, retains its completed object during deferred recording and promotes that same object into chat. Cancellation and retirement stop original loaders, and teardown restores the instance methods when the observer still owns them. Direct recordBashResult also creates an original completed row. Host/ExtensionUIContext info, warning and error notices add original Spacer/ThemedText nodes in chat chronology; adjacent info at the same message position updates the existing native node. Notices retain original padding and do not enter SDK messages or JSONL.

This category followed whole-group implementation, grouped verification and consolidated repairs. The initial complete source run passed **1054/1054**, before the image projection/type/fixture repairs and the new image regression. The repair source group passed 465/466: its sole failure read desktop projection output before the queued expansion update. Product code is unchanged by the final source-test repair. No fresh final full source or complete default browser suite is claimed.

The initial browser group passed 33/41. Bash assertions expected an exact paragraph although command and output share one paragraph; generic image placeholders added a duplicate logical preview line while the image was already displayed in DOM. The tool-context click was covered by the shared fixture's replacement footer after earlier workflows accumulated many statuses; its control helper now restores Pi's default footer. The following narrow case's busy rejection was a consequence of that unfinished wide case. Typed SDK callback/receiver parameters and private-field casts repair TypeScript errors. The added source regression verifies desktop image projection, image visibility changes, direct original expansion and unchanged full native results/messages.

One final type-checked unbundled build passes, with all three backend artifacts updated. All **seven** native groups have passing evidence against that executable: application-components, tool-context, tool-shell, tool-display, shared-tui and terminal pass in the first sequential batch; native-execution (tools/bash/notices) passes after its test-only readiness repair. That first batch exits 1 because the new workflow reaches its default 5-second assertion before initialization completes. The browser overlap does not increase the 41 distinct scenarios. No product startup correction or definitive delay cause is claimed. Representative desktop, narrow and native screenshots were inspected. ## Original application messages, footer and status (2026-10-07)

The shared application tree now retains original SDK UserMessageComponent, AssistantMessageComponent, compaction/branch summary and skill invocation components, completed BashExecutionComponent objects, FooterComponent and working/retry/compaction/branch-summary indicators. Streamed assistant completion preserves native object identity; unchanged snapshots preserve direct native content, padding and expansion mutations. Settings/transformer/theme changes invalidate native Markdown caches. Default and replacement footers share one original FooterDataProvider per generation; explicit extension statuses are independent of working-message text. The active original indicator embeds in the default CustomEditor or mounts in the status region for an editor without embedding; replacement, hiding and retirement release its timers.

FooterComponent is the 22nd generic mapped type. It consumes the complete original render output as styled desktop text, preserving subclass additions, empty frames, identity and lifetime without duplicate frames or terminal fallback. Shared provider disposal belongs to the generation, so replacing a footer does not retire its provider. Public compaction start/end events distinguish compaction from Pi's branch navigation in isCompacting, including the first summary attempt. Real SDK navigation and compact calls verify both original indicator classes and cleanup.

## Original default editor and application roots (2026-10-07)

This category follows implementation of the whole group, grouped validation, consolidated repairs and grouped revalidation. The default composer now uses an original SDK `CustomEditor` through the generic component mapping, with the shared TUI, configured keybindings, padding, completion height and working-status embedding. Replacing it with an extension editor retains the default object; restoring it transfers expanded text while retaining history and configuration. History initializes once per original object. Autocomplete replacements reach both active and retained editors. Direct SDK mutation remains authoritative. The previous `text`/`selection` transport aliases resolve to current generic control actions before ordinary action validation and dispatch; instance/lifecycle/availability guards remain active.

The native application tree now has seven original Container roots: document, pending, status, above-editor widgets, editor, below-editor widgets and footer. The document nests header, resources and chat containers. Current resources, builtin messages/header/footer, queues, working state and string widgets use stable native Text bridges; extension factory roots remain their original components. Empty/hidden working state and removed widgets leave no stale text. Extension children are retained independently. Recursive visibility respects a removed document/root, and global clear does not silently reconstruct roots. This does **not** establish the original builtin message/FooterComponent/StatusIndicator instances.

Consolidated repairs preserve paste bodies while transferring expanded drafts, use Pi's actual default input engine, preserve ranges for consumed input/unchanged key releases, and clear accepted follow-up text synchronously before activity is emitted. Async clipboard replacement therefore retains its selection until insertion. ## Shared extension TUI runtime (2026-10-07)

This category was implemented together, verified in groups, and repaired after collecting each group's results. Factories and render contexts now receive one stable TUI proxy and shared native renderer/terminal per desktop generation. Original Pi Container instances host the header, messages, above-editor, editor, below-editor and footer slots. Direct registrations use a generic region in native order, retain duplicate occurrences, and survive their creator's retirement or failure. Custom and direct overlays share the original native stack. Global clear removes application containers as well; stop/start pauses/resumes mapped regions and the default editor while retaining their instances. Cross-factory input passes through the shared listener pipeline once and waits for the recipient before replying. Borrowed original components retain their owning parent's disposal authority.

Native validation exposed a focus race that browser fill acknowledgements alone could not solve: command text could reach another field. Desktop focus notifications now carry the actual leaf action, surface instance and observed focus revision. They synchronize that leaf directly without first activating a native overlay root, which would arm Pi's blocked-focus restoration and alter a subsequent SDK setFocus call. They also acknowledge browser focus without echoing an application-level first-control focus command. Overlay fallback focus preserves an existing control inside the overlay; delayed focus events/frames respect newer DOM focus. Late editor acknowledgements update their old field without reclaiming focus. Listener-driven forwarding retains its new explicit SDK request. SDK overlay-handle activation/restoration remains native. The registration workflow explicitly focuses its command, waits two animation frames, checks focus, then verifies SDK text/control-version acknowledgement before submission. Two source cases cover custom/direct overlay leaf focus, subsequent SDK focus, stale revisions/instances, unavailable/removed controls and explicit native handle activation.

## Shared terminal presentation state (2026-10-06)

This category was implemented and verified together, with consolidated repairs after each grouped result. Direct progress has one last-writer state; opaque/PTY OSC effect owners retain their independent existing lifetimes.

Color queries keep Pi's original timeout, partial-result and late-reply parser behavior across factory retirement. Options are captured synchronously at call entry, so later mutations cannot replace the timeout or callback. Late callbacks use the original bare invocation with an undefined receiver. Generation retirement settles pending queries, cancels their timers/subscriptions/progress and prevents old handles from affecting a successor. Nine new source cases cover shared flags/listeners/window state, native differential traces, failed factories, explicit unsubscribe, retained handles, query options and generation cancellation. ## Keyboard event phases (2026-10-06)

This category was implemented together, verified as a group, and repaired together. Desktop inputs now encode press/repeat/release phases for Pi's original parsers. Key-only desktop controllers recover transformed release/repeat metadata; ordinary event/result shapes retain their prior fields.

Release events do not synchronize stale browser text, selection or layout, including when a global listener transforms a raw release into a press. A release with unchanged original text/caret returns no editor transaction, preserving browser selection changes such as Ctrl+A. Repeated printable input replaces selected text through the original Pi Input/Editor path. Control/Meta letter identity remains normalized, and the desktop modal editor recognizes repeated normal-mode character commands.

Opaque component xterm input now joins the same ordered keyboard/text path. Its helper textarea is marked raw, and xterm's own key/composition/paste handlers cannot deliver a second copy of the captured input. Original component handlers, focus and results remain live. ## Custom message and entry renderers (2026-10-06)

This category was implemented together, then verified and repaired as a group. The shared bridge maps Pi's original `CustomMessageComponent` and `CustomEntryComponent` as whole component trees. ## Transcript content width and layout context (2026-10-06)

This category was implemented together, then verified and repaired as a group. Transcript Markdown transformers and Mermaid now receive columns measured from the actual desktop message content, independently for ordinary text and thinking. An inert hidden layout probe follows the transcript's maximum width, responsive padding/avatar/gaps, output padding, thinking border/padding, monospace glyph size and live scrollbar. Scrollbar space is removed before the maximum-width constraint is applied. The probe is available before the first message and observes container/panel/font changes without depending on whole-window resize events.

Desktop prompt and thinking-visibility requests await the latest measurement, including when ResizeObserver delivery has not happened yet. Geometry updates serialize independently of extension input. The host accepts only valid integer widths for its current backend/session and rejects measurements during replacement or before initialization. The original Pi Markdown receives measured available columns plus its output padding, which it then subtracts according to the original transformer contract. Headless/unmeasured sessions retain the previous terminal-viewport fallback. Standard extension viewport sizing and xterm remain independent; Pi 1.0.0 is unchanged.

## Raw terminal title, progress and clipboard effects (2026-10-06)

This category was implemented, verified and repaired as a group. xterm's OSC parser now forwards raw OSC 0/2 titles, OSC 9;4 progress and OSC 52 clipboard writes to the desktop host. Standalone original `ProcessTerminal` and opaque custom component output use the same effect contract; mapped factory title/progress callbacks retain their existing path. Pi 1.0.0 is unchanged. Clipboard decoding validates base64 and UTF-8 and retains Pi's 100,000 encoded-character limit (75,000 UTF-8 bytes), including Unicode and empty writes. Clipboard queries and non-clipboard selections are outside this write-only bridge.

PTY deliveries carry the supervisor's terminal ID, output sequence and effect index. A high-water mark ignores history replay, duplicate effects and retired terminals; future output sequences reject. Opaque components carry an authoritative data revision and retain effect indices through unchanged renders and browser remounts, with existing surface/instance guards. Effects serialize separately from xterm parsing and PTY input. A real inherited synchronous child emits title/progress/copy while blocking the SDK worker; keyboard input still reaches it, then the queued effects complete. Reset/disposal retires queued output/effects. Progress cleanup releases its factory owner while preserving an independent active owner.

Legacy example adapters are disabled. The final frontend runtime behavior matches the first broader run; the later product edit only narrows a TypeScript parameter type. ## Workspace bootstrap and browser recovery (2026-10-06)

This category was implemented and verified as a group. The real SDK runtime was deliberately held absent during workspace replacement while the browser received the previous session's snapshot. That reproduces the workspace-not-initialized notices: draft restoration, session-list loading and extension viewport reporting can race initialization. Those automatic requests now wait for a connected, initialized workspace. Delayed editor updates carry the session ID, and an old snapshot cannot replace the selected workspace in local storage during bootstrap. Attachments remain intact when the same workspace becomes ready again.

Browser HTTP/SSE authentication now shares concurrent token requests, renews credentials on reconnect and rebuilds the event stream with bounded retry delays. An unauthorized action is retried once because the server rejects it before SDK invocation; accepted actions and network/server failures are not replayed. A changed backend credential reinitializes the SDK workspace and retires old dialogs/state. Connection generations reject late action responses from the previous backend. Unsubscribe and graceful SDK shutdown cancel reconnects. xterm rereads terminal history/query state on reconnect and resets its sequence for a replacement backend.

Mapped extensions remain interactive while initialization is pending. A real extension's `session_start` hook opens an original Pi Input and awaits its submission. Grouped validation exposed that gating the keyboard bridge on full workspace readiness would prevent Enter from completing this hook. The bridge therefore remains available while connected; only automatic workspace synchronization waits for initialization. The new desktop/narrow cases verify the original input callback and completed initialization without notices.

Final focused source coverage passes 12/12, including shared token renewal, safe action retry, retired SSE suppression, reconnect cancellation, native dialog replay, terminal probe serialization and an actual synchronous inherited PTY child. Final browser coverage passes 30/30 (2.0m): ten new bootstrap/recovery/input cases at 1440px/760px, plus existing default editor, project trust, graceful shutdown and xterm cases. The first grouped repair also exposed a missing `terminal.snapshot` test-supervisor operation and a hidden sidebar in one narrow test; the fixture now mirrors the real supervisor and opens the sidebar before its command. Initial failure logs remain under `.local/connection-recovery-*-before*.log`.

The corrected category has one final unbundled desktop build, including TypeScript checking; the earlier build before the startup-input repair remains historical. Existing backend/Node/dependency artifacts are reused because only the frontend changed. No dependency installation or installer rebuild was performed. The SDK audit retains 458 declaration exports, 156 unchanged native exports, 38 public constructors and 16 declaration static members. Verification logs use `.local/connection-recovery-{source,ui,native-build,audit}.log`.

The final executable passes three sequential native groups: all four SDK lifecycle workflows, real xterm/PTY input/resize and inherited synchronous child callbacks, and startup session-only project trust. Logs are `.local/connection-recovery-native{,-sdk-lifecycle,-terminal,-project-trust-startup}.log`. `.local/connection-recovery-receipt.json` verifies log/source/artifact hashes and live direct/proxy preview authentication. Backend copies retain SHA-256 `18C6DC968760267275C5ED19ED3C34B9F2CAD6470CE10A7F8EC8A578AEBB1FAC`; the receipt records the final executable identity. ## Default npm discovery and legacy global packages (2026-10-06)

This category was collected and verified as a group. Three workflows leave public `npmCommand` unset and supply isolated npm configuration through the worker environment. On Windows, the original SDK discovers the installed npm through PATH. Default desktop user/project install/update/remove works with real scoped archives and dependency-backed extension commands. An actual `npm install --global` writes only into a private fixture prefix, never the user's global package directory.

Configured legacy global packages resolve through original `npm root -g` lookup and load without downloading another copy. Available updates create a managed user installation that takes lookup precedence. Exact versions remain fixed; changing the configured exact/range target reconciles the managed version while retaining the legacy manifest/extension file hashes and its original version. Removing the configured managed package preserves the old global package, removes its commands from the hosted session and leaves it available to explicit native lookup. Changing public `npmCommand` to an empty alternate global prefix and back refreshes that lookup correctly.

Source integration passes 10/10 (206.2s), consisting of the three new workflows and seven preceding npm/Git regressions. Final browser coverage passes 2/2 (1.8m), running all three workflows at both 1440px/760px. Native `--sdk-package-environment` passes all three workflows, original SDK prompt recovery and desktop editing. Final TypeScript checking passes. Logs are `.local/sdk-package-environment-{integration,ui,native,check-final}.log`. The preceding 458/156 export identity audit remains historical; no fresh full integration suite or new export audit is claimed.

The first browser/native groups fail their final legacy-version workflow. The tests reused one resource-loader instance while changing each fixture's npm prefix; Pi caches its global npm root by command. The loader therefore kept the preceding prefix and installed a managed copy instead of discovering the next fixture's legacy package. Each browser/native workflow now initializes an independent empty workspace/resource loader and restores the original workspace before closing its fixture. The SDK fixture assertions and production code are unchanged. Initial logs remain in `.local/sdk-package-environment-{ui,native}-before-prefix-isolation.log`. This is validation isolation, not an established desktop or Pi defect.

`.local/sdk-package-environment-receipt.json` verifies the completed logs, test-source hashes and reused executable/backend/Node hashes against the preceding network receipt. ## SDK npm/Git acquisition and updates (2026-10-06)

This category was collected, verified and repaired as a group. An isolated loopback npm registry serves actual npm-packed archives, including a scoped package and a production dependency. A dumb HTTP Git remote serves a real repository with changing commits and tags. The tests invoke the original SDK and real npm/Git subprocesses; Pi 1.0.0 and the product backend remain unchanged. Fixtures select the externally installed npm CLI through public `npmCommand` settings with isolated configuration/cache; this category does not establish a bundled npm installation or default npm command discovery.

Four user/project workflows combine original `installAndPersist`/`removeAndPersist`, configured-source persistence and resource metadata with desktop `packages.install`/`packages.update`/`packages.remove`. Original extension commands load their installed dependency and report the installed version before and after update/reload. Three further workflows exercise exact/range versions, batched npm updates, project precedence/deltas, temporary npm/Git isolation, pinned Git reference reconciliation, dirty/untracked checkout cleanup, missing dependency repair, floating temporary Git refresh, unavailable-remote cache fallback and `PI_OFFLINE` request suppression. Failure recovery covers missing-source decisions/rejection identity, registry failures, failed clone dependency installation cleanup, update markers, dependency installation retry and Git update failures.

The initial source run passes 1/7. Its fixture reused the packing cache for installation, so the install did not download an archive; it also assumed npm `--omit=dev` would not resolve development dependency metadata. Packing now has its own cache, and the Git fixture checks that its published development dependency is absent from the installed tree. The next source run passes 6/7; its remaining failure, also reproduced in browser/native, treats `getInstalledPath()` before installation as a string, although Pi returns `undefined`. The corrected failure workflow verifies that missing result and obtains the path after successful installation. These were validation assumptions, not established product defects. Export identity retains 458 declaration exports and 156 unchanged native exports; the inventory retains 41 declared classes, 38 public constructors and 16 declaration static members, including 15 on runtime exports.

## Project trust bootstrap (2026-10-06)

This category was implemented together, then validated and repaired as a group. The host now delegates to Pi's original project-trust resolver during resource-loader bootstrap. Global extensions receive `project_trust` before project settings, packages and extensions are loaded, and the original bootstrap extensions are reused. Handler precedence, remembered decisions, `defaultProjectTrust`, parent-folder inheritance and session-only choices retain Pi's behavior. Empty projects need no grant. Temporary decisions are cached per cwd for this host, and explicit `trust.set` updates the cache. Caller-provided `projectTrustContextFactory` contexts reach the resolver unchanged. Pi 1.0.0 remains pinned and unmodified; access to its internal resolver has an explicit version guard.

Trust dialogs use the host lifetime, so replacing a session cannot cancel its successor's bootstrap prompts. Host shutdown cancels the prompts before project loading resumes. Host-bound dialog input runs before the session requirement and independently of retired session input listeners/shortcuts. This fixes the grouped browser regression where text typed during workspace replacement was rejected because no SDK session existed yet. Ordinary session dialogs retain their existing input dispatch order. Keybindings are loaded before trust bootstrap.

The initial focused run passes 1/15 and fails 14/15 (`.local/project-trust-before.log`). Final full integration coverage passes 912/912 (147.0s), including 19 project-trust cases. Affected browser coverage passes 50/50 (4.1m), including five trust workflows at both 1440px and 760px. The first browser attempt used an incorrect confirmation-button label; the second exposed lost bootstrap input, with later failures cascading from its pending initialization. The fixture label, pending-dialog cleanup and product input dispatch were corrected before the final grouped run. A final TypeScript error concerned the extracted helper's missing return-type fields; its type was corrected without changing runtime behavior, and the consolidated build's TypeScript check passes. Logs are `.local/project-trust-{all-tests-final,ui-complete,native-build}.log`. Trust screenshots at both widths were inspected.

Sequential staged-runtime coverage passes 27/27 (115.9s), covering transport results/rejections, construction/shutdown, socket resets and inherited subprocess input/resize (`.local/project-trust-staged.log`). Staged trust behavior is covered by the native executable's resource backend, rather than by running the in-process trust tests against source again. ## Running SDK operation drain (2026-10-06)

This category was implemented together and validated as a group. Invoked `withSdk` callbacks and named SDK operations now join `RuntimeLifecycle` shutdown. Closing signals cancellation before waiting and retains the original runtime/session until admitted callbacks return or reject. Direct results, functions and rejection identity remain intact. Nested callbacks awaiting their own shutdown exclude their ancestry while concurrent callbacks still join. `sdk.run` module preparation remains cancellable outside the drain; its admitted callback joins through `withSdk`. A callback that ignores cancellation and never settles can prevent normal shutdown. Pi 1.0.0 and its exports/prototypes remain unchanged.

## Runtime callback composition (2026-10-06)

This category was implemented together and then validated as a group. Calling the public `AgentSessionRuntime.setRebindSession()` or `setBeforeSessionInvalidate()` previously replaced the desktop's independent session-binding or cleanup callback, including when callers cleared their callback with `undefined`. The initial probe records 12 failures (`.local/runtime-callbacks-before.log`): eleven demonstrate lost desktop UI/cleanup, while the workspace case also used the wrong snapshot field. That assertion now reads `snapshot.editor.text`; not all twelve failures are claimed as independent product regressions.

`backend/runtime-callbacks.ts` composes caller hooks on hosted runtime instances. Setting, replacing or clearing them retains desktop binding and cleanup across original `newSession`, `switchSession`, `fork` and `importFromJsonl` operations. Desktop binding runs before caller rebind and per-operation `withSession`. Caller before-invalidate runs before desktop cleanup, with cleanup in `finally` if the caller throws. Pi's synchronous, non-awaited before-invalidate semantics remain intact. Each rebind invocation captures its caller before awaiting desktop binding, so concurrent replacement takes effect on the next invocation. Original runtime receivers, setter returns and caller errors are retained. Retirement detaches the host, restores owned descriptors and the latest caller callbacks, and respects later setter replacements or frozen owned methods. Frozen SDK state may retain a detached composite that can call only the caller hook. Pi 1.0.0 and its prototypes remain unchanged.

Sixteen new integration cases cover all four transitions, hook installation/replacement/clearing, non-awaited before-invalidate Promises, in-flight callback replacement, caller-requested shutdown, stale workspace isolation, borrowed setters, descriptors, frozen/replaced methods and cleanup rejection identity. The full integration suite passes 882/882 (`.local/runtime-callbacks-all-tests.log`, 143.3s); TypeScript checking passes (`.local/runtime-callbacks-check.log` and the native build). All 47 affected browser cases pass (`.local/runtime-callbacks-ui-final.log`, 2.6m), including the four callback workflows, authentication, SDK errors/lifecycle, editors, dialogs, custom-close/overlay lifetime, shutdown and all five xterm cases. The first browser run passes 46/47: its initial navigation takes approximately 56 seconds and consumes almost all of the 60-second test budget before authentication begins. The grouped rerun uses a 120-second test budget and passes; no product startup correction is claimed. Both runs use legacy presentations disabled.

One unbundled native build passes (`.local/runtime-callbacks-native-build.log`, Rust 1m27s). The built WebView2 application passes all four callback workflows with legacy presentations disabled, four SDK lifecycle workflows, window-close/SDK-context/overlapping shutdown and actual xterm/PTY synchronous inherited-child input/resize plus original component callbacks (`.local/runtime-callbacks-native-{runtime-callbacks,sdk-lifecycle,sdk-shutdown-window,sdk-shutdown,sdk-shutdown-overlap,terminal}.log`). After native validation, all 27 staged-runtime cases pass sequentially (`.local/runtime-callbacks-staged.log`, 111.1s): 23 actual staged subprocess cases and four in-process SDK cases. The callback installation probe runs inside each of the four existing transport matrices. The SDK identity audit passes 458 declaration exports and 156 unchanged native exports (`.local/runtime-callbacks-sdk-audit.log`).

Source, staged and native-resource backend copies share SHA-256 `08E7CA17E7D849B81D44F9C6EAE06D5A1875BA16E79AB5471550DCC2BFF2AEBB`. Staged/native Node copies share SHA-256 `35EBB74DA6ACB56FDF570DE64F1AE510D6D18CA09DA494A3E1AC87EDD32D263B`; the tested executable has SHA-256 `2FB86BDD6CB13BD8E3B2F309E7701CEB58A7EE7CB88C2E4C82B41EA137A642F0`. The receipt checks completed validation logs before recording these identities (`.local/runtime-callbacks-artifact-hashes.json`). The installer was not rebuilt and there are no later product edits.

## SDK errors and result transport (2026-10-06)

This category was implemented together, validated as a group and repaired as a group. Native/stdio action rejection previously assumed `error.message`: a `null` rejection could throw again while reporting failure, leaving its original request unanswered. Empty error strings were also treated as success by the PTY receiver and HTTP client. Shared safe error formatting now handles non-Error throws and objects that cannot convert to strings. Protocol errors retain their request IDs, empty errors reject correctly and the actual desktop client rejects with an `Error` consistently on HTTP and Tauri. Direct `withSdk` calls retain the original rejection identity.

Void results cross each transport as `null`. Top-level function/symbol results reject explicitly; BigInt, cyclic data and throwing `toJSON` implementations produce an error response and leave the transport available for subsequent requests. Native SDK objects, functions and callbacks remain available inside Node. Component rendering, invalidation, asynchronous mounting and cleanup use safe error formatting, so an unprintable exception cannot break its error presentation or prevent cleanup publication.

Final product source passes 866/866 full integration cases (`.local/sdk-transport-all-tests-final.log`, 114.0s), including four real transport matrices and direct SDK/extension error recovery. All 27 affected browser cases pass (`.local/sdk-transport-ui-final.log`, 2.4m), covering the actual desktop client error/result matrix, notices, SDK lifecycle, authentication, dialogs, default editor, shutdown and all five xterm cases. The first integration run passes 865/866: its new native test used a UUID instead of the required 32-character hexadecimal channel token. Correcting that fixture produces the final passing run. An unprintable component-error regression was added with the grouped error-reporting correction.

One unbundled native build passes (`.local/sdk-transport-native-build.log`, Rust 1m12s). The built WebView2 application passes the SDK error/result matrix, four SDK lifecycle workflows, overlapping shutdown and xterm/PTY synchronous child input/resize plus original component callbacks (`.local/sdk-transport-native-{sdk-transport,sdk-lifecycle,sdk-shutdown-overlap,terminal}.log`). The native error workflow bundles the actual final-source desktop client for invocation within the real WebView2 context; the product UI receives and displays its original extension notice. Pi 1.0.0 remains unchanged: 458 declaration exports and 156 native exports pass the identity audit (`.local/sdk-transport-sdk-audit.log`).

The final staged category passes 27/27 sequential cases (`.local/sdk-transport-staged-complete.log`, 102.2s): 23 actual staged subprocess cases and four in-process original SDK cases, including socket reset and the four error/result matrices. A preceding parallel run has HTTP readiness and stdio initialization timeouts; its completed lifecycle test worker retained a local fixture service after failed startup and was stopped after its process ownership was verified. A serial 26-case attempt then passes 25/26 with an unlabelled HTTP/PTY request timeout. The test harness now closes its fixture on failed startup, gives readiness/initialization the backend's 30-second startup budget, preserves 10-second subsequent request limits and names failed HTTP operations. These are test changes after the full source/browser run; no product startup correction or definitive cause of the earlier slow starts is claimed. Final TypeScript checking passes (`.local/sdk-transport-check-final.log`); no further native build or installer packaging was needed for those harness-only edits.

Staged/native Node copies share SHA-256 `35EBB74DA6ACB56FDF570DE64F1AE510D6D18CA09DA494A3E1AC87EDD32D263B`; the tested executable has SHA-256 `AAEAED12BFF158F85D65599B4907B8DCC6EE8C6877B3A5D915F4D9E2DE83E465` (`.local/sdk-transport-artifact-hashes.json`). ## SDK operation and runtime lifecycle (2026-10-06)

This category was implemented together and then validated as a group. `RuntimeLifecycle` tracks initialization, desktop mutations, direct SDK operations and hosted runtime transitions while preserving original returned values and Promise identity. Operation ancestry allows callbacks to await host shutdown without a dependency cycle; other pending work still joins cleanup. Factories completing after self-requested closure are disposed and cannot revive the host. Hosted `newSession`, `switchSession`, `fork` and `importFromJsonl` are bound on the runtime instance only; borrowed receivers delegate to the original methods and owned descriptors restore during cleanup, including cleanup failure. Pi 1.0.0 and its prototypes remain unchanged.

SDK operation IDs are reserved before asynchronous module preparation. Admission checks cancellation, captured runtime/session and project trust before and after import. Closed or pre-aborted `withSdk` calls reject before invocation, owner cancellation is available and explicit caller abort reasons retain identity. Running callbacks retain their original result/cancellation behavior. Seventeen new integration cases cover preparation cancellation, duplicate IDs, changed session/workspace/trust, all four direct transitions, shutdown callers, late factories, return identity and descriptor ownership.

The final full integration suite passes 861/861 (`.local/sdk-lifecycle-all-tests-complete.log`, 131.4s); final TypeScript checking passes (`.local/sdk-lifecycle-check-final.log`). Two preceding attempts exposed test setup that used fire-and-forget desktop prompt handling before fork/import. Awaiting the original SDK prompt fixes the fixture; neither preceding attempt is a passing full-suite result. All 23 affected browser cases pass in one completed run (`.local/sdk-lifecycle-ui.log`, 1.7m), including four new SDK workflows, authentication, editor/session lifecycle, shutdown and all five xterm cases with legacy presentations disabled.

One unbundled native build passes (`.local/sdk-lifecycle-native-build.log`, Rust 1m27s). Actual WebView2/native transport passes all four SDK operation workflows (`.local/sdk-lifecycle-native-sdk-lifecycle.log`), window-close, SDK-context and overlapping shutdown, and xterm/PTY synchronous child input/resize plus original component callbacks (`.local/sdk-lifecycle-native-{sdk-shutdown-window,sdk-shutdown,sdk-shutdown-overlap,terminal}.log`). All 22 staged-runtime cases pass sequentially after native verification (`.local/sdk-lifecycle-staged.log`, 89.7s). No later product change precedes this receipt.

The source, staged and native-resource backend copies share SHA-256 `305A3F1D39839207EADDBB33662D7288796EDCBBC7477836076DAA4C43BC441B`. Staged/native Node copies share SHA-256 `35EBB74DA6ACB56FDF570DE64F1AE510D6D18CA09DA494A3E1AC87EDD32D263B`; the tested executable has SHA-256 `D28B9539F7ABAB7118E1D306521335BFC0DCD19BDA8FE9949FEBF9B9F816C6A0` (`.local/sdk-lifecycle-artifact-hashes.json`). The installer was not rebuilt.

## Runtime and communication lifecycle (2026-10-06)

This category is implemented together and validated as a group, following the user's request to avoid per-feature checks and repeated packaging. HTTP `shutdown` now joins host disposal, sends its response, drains existing HTTP/SSE responses and exits the supervisor. Concurrent shutdown callers share cleanup. SDK-context cleanup failures publish one shutdown event with `exitCode: 1` and terminate with an error instead of leaving the transport alive. Native/stdio disconnect and error handlers observe disposal failures without introducing an unhandled rejected Promise.

`TerminalHost` now waits for actual PTY exit after a shutdown acknowledgement. Startup exit rejects authentication waiters immediately; startup timers, unused sockets and listeners are released on failure. Authenticated channel closure clears the stale socket and rejects outstanding requests. The supervisor remains available for terminal history after a connected worker exits or loses its SDK channel; explicit supervisor shutdown then joins worker cleanup and reports its exit failure. Repeated shutdown joins the same disposal Promise. A context-triggered shutdown event no longer terminates the outer protocol before its joined shutdown response and PTY exit.

`DesktopHost` joins pending initialization and mutations before final runtime disposal, guards late binding, and memoizes disposal per original SDK runtime. Initial construction, workspace replacement and new-session construction overlapping shutdown have real original-runtime regressions. Native Tauri registration and disconnect draining share a lock and connection state, so late requests fail immediately. A request guard releases callbacks on future cancellation, write failure or timeout; SDK-requested native exit waits for the protocol reader to drain.

The final full integration run passes 844/844 (`.local/runtime-lifecycle-all-tests-final.log`, 121.1s), including nine new lifecycle cases and all 11 native/stdio transport variants. HTTP tests use direct SDK and actual PTY workers, asynchronous original extension cleanup, repeated shutdown, abort-aware pending operations, SSE draining, actual worker-PID termination, startup failure and unexpected worker exit. The socket-reset regression retains terminal-history access and verifies one asynchronous cleanup marker plus explicit supervisor exit. TypeScript and all four Rust checks pass (`.local/runtime-lifecycle-check-final.log`, `.local/runtime-lifecycle-rust-final.log`).

The first full integration attempt was interrupted and is not a completed full-suite result. Its native overlap case exposed premature protocol termination; joining host disposal before finishing the outer protocol corrects that case. Its TypeScript check also found an initialization Promise declaration mismatch, which is corrected in final source.

All 17 selected browser cases pass in one completed run with legacy presentations disabled (`.local/runtime-lifecycle-ui-final.log`, 1.3m): desktop SDK workflows, default editor/session drafts, pause/resume lifecycle, closed-state presentation and all five real xterm cases. The initial local Playwright configuration used its own directory as the server working directory and did not start; explicitly setting the repository working directory resolves that test-configuration error without a product change. Desktop xterm and narrow default-editor screenshots were inspected. This is affected coverage, not a fresh complete default browser suite.

One unbundled desktop release build passes (`.local/runtime-lifecycle-native-build.log`, Rust 1m19s). Its actual WebView2/native transport passes window-close, SDK-context and overlapping shutdown, awaiting exactly one original asynchronous cleanup hook in each case. The window-close hook lasts 4.5 seconds, exceeding the emergency-exit fallback. ## Overlay handle presentation and component lifetime (2026-10-06)

The unchanged Pi `InteractiveMode.showExtensionCustom` and actual original `TuiMainScreen.showOverlay` provide the differential reference for overlay handles. Eight public native/opaque custom-UI cases and four direct native/opaque overlay cases reproduce differences before this correction (`.local/overlay-lifecycle-before.log`, 14 failures including their two parent groups). Desktop `hide()` completed the custom result and disposed its component; direct removal also disposed its original component on the next refresh. The original Pi handle removes the presentation while leaving the custom result and component alive. The reference uses Pi's original SDK prompt wrapper on both public paths, rather than a fake overlay handle.

`DesktopUIRegistry` now detaches the presentation independently of completion. Detached surfaces reject input, actions and measurements while retaining their mounted lifecycle until `done`, cancellation or teardown. `component-mapping.ts` removes the original native overlay and retains its component tree without rendering the detached overlay; `hideOverlay()` selects the latest attached overlay. Explicit hidden state remains separate from responsive visibility, `focus()` does not reveal a hidden or removed presentation, and restoring a temporarily hidden capturing overlay restores focus. The isolated detach/nested/state probes are retained in `.local/overlay-lifecycle-{detach,nested,state}.log`. A queued-input regression additionally verifies that events accepted before removal cannot invoke a later queued input/action callback after removal.

## Asynchronous captured disposal callbacks (2026-10-06)

Captured guards previously lost their cleanup scope after the original callback returned. Microtasks, timers and `await` then skipped the saved original; an asynchronous original parent could also clean a child that the desktop had retained. The first reproduction reports 11 failures out of 12 tests (`.local/component-async-before.log`). The original cross-factory case passed that first probe; its captured alias was later strengthened to retain the peer's guard before retirement, and the final strengthened case passes.

`component-disposal.ts` now uses Node `AsyncLocalStorage` for mounted ownership and callback invocation chains. Each cleanup captures its factory's current records, descendant graph, retained components and cleanup delegation. Continuations keep that snapshot even after retirement, preserving old ownership without redirecting into a later mount. A new release snapshots the current graph. Reentrant cleanup scopes remain independent, including factories sharing the same original method. Callback result memoization preserves original values/Promises and recursive/duplicate guarding. Exception unwinding restores the caller's context. Unscoped retired guards remain inert; locked/frozen descriptors retain their original values and receivers. No Pi source/package edit, global prototype patch or extension-specific adapter is added.

The final focused suite passes 59/59 (`.local/component-async-source-final.log`): 15 asynchronous cases plus the 44 preceding disposal/remount/wrapper cases. Desktop and narrow screenshots were inspected. This is affected-suite evidence, not a fresh complete default browser suite. ## Captured disposal wrappers (2026-10-06)

An extension can save a mounted component's current `dispose`, replace it with its own wrapper and call the saved function from that wrapper. The previous tracker reused one mutable guard, so entering the new wrapper marked the mount disposed and the captured guard skipped the original callback. Multi-layer wrappers lost their inner callbacks too. Five tracker variants and a real Pi Input callback reproduce the failure before the correction (`.local/component-wrapper-before.log`, seven failing reported tests including their group).

Each guard now retains an immutable snapshot of its original callback and descriptor. A replacement receives a new guard. During an original synchronous cleanup chain, captured guards invoke their saved callback in the current mount's scope, including callbacks captured from a retired mount. A per-chain callback set prevents recursion and duplicate guarded calls. Outside such a chain, retired guards remain inert. Peer restoration creates a fresh guard for the current replacement, preserving later cleanup, callback receivers and original descriptor flags. Locked/frozen wrappers retain their properties; their saved guards can still reach the original callback during desktop cleanup. This adds no Pi package edit, global prototype patch or extension-specific adapter.

The final focused suite passes 44/44 (`.local/component-wrapper-source-final.log`): 11 new wrapper cases plus 33 preceding remount/disposal cases. The seven wrapper modes cover single/multi-layer callbacks, successive mounts, newly mounted/existing peers, post-guard freezing and freezing the replacement itself. Additional cases cover duplicate/recursive calls, throwing originals with `finally` cleanup, and three real Pi Input dialogs whose original input callback installs a wrapper. Assertions verify callback order, receivers, restoration, subsequent cleanup and stale guards. Initial TypeScript issues in an optional captured callback and a throwing callback inferred as `never` were corrected in the tests. Final TypeScript checking passes (`.local/component-wrapper-check-final.log`).

The full integration suite passes 764/764 (`.local/component-wrapper-all-tests-final.log`). The unchanged SDK audit retains 458 declaration exports and 156 runtime exports (`.local/component-wrapper-sdk-audit.log`). Export retention is not proof of every capability's behavior. This wrapper correction covers synchronous original callbacks; deferred calls through captured guards after awaiting/scheduling work have not been verified. Full SDK completion remains open, including the remaining ownership, geometry, external-service, physical-input, installed-copy and SDK-upgrade requirements in the completion review.

This is affected-suite evidence, not a fresh complete default browser suite. ## Component reuse across factories (2026-10-06)

Original Pi header/footer/custom surfaces call the current component's `dispose` on each close. Reusing that original object in a later factory starts another cleanup lifecycle. The desktop previously left its guard installed, so writable/configurable/inherited methods ran only on the first close. Two overlapping factories repeatedly wrapped each other's guard and could omit the original cleanup entirely. `.local/component-remount-cleanup-before.log` retains the failing real-SDK reproductions.

`backend/component-disposal.ts` now records disposal per mount. Instance guards carry private WeakMap metadata identifying their original target and callback, so another factory can unwrap a known desktop guard rather than wrapping it recursively. Retirement restores the original own method, removes an inherited-method shadow or reinstalls another active factory's guard. Callback-installed replacements and descriptor flag changes survive retirement. Captured retired guards cannot dispose a successor lifecycle. Locked/accessor/nonextensible objects retain their existing descriptors and original receivers.

Original parent cleanup calls retain their factory's scope when a shared child currently has another factory's guard. Reentrant release establishes its own scope and restores the caller's scope afterwards; a still-active disposed guard remains available to suppress repeated calls inside that caller. A later reproduction caught a previously cleaned active guard swallowing a different parent's call (`.local/component-remount-owner-before.log`). Scope routing now precedes that mount's disposed check. A component frozen after initial mounting can preserve a nonreplaceable old guard; desktop cleanup resolves its known original callback without writing the frozen descriptor (`.local/component-remount-frozen-before.log`). This does not intercept arbitrary direct calls through methods frozen around a retired guard.

The final focused suite passes 33/33, comprising 15 remount cases and the 18 preceding disposal cases (`.local/component-remount-source-final.log`). It exercises real Pi Input objects, writable/configurable/inherited/getter/locked methods, frozen render-only roots, overlapping header/footer factories, stale callbacks, replacement methods, cleanup errors, original parent calls, reentrant cleanup stages and post-mount freezing. The initial remount test incorrectly concatenated a control key and text into one Pi input event; it was corrected to send the original key events separately before collecting the behavioral failure log. Initial TypeScript failures in closure narrowing and an inferred `never` test callback were corrected.

The final full integration suite passes 753/753 (`.local/component-remount-all-tests-final.log`). The earlier 751-case run and first native build preceded the further scope and freezing corrections and are intermediate evidence. Final TypeScript checking and native rebuilding pass (`.local/component-remount-native-build-final.log`; Rust compilation 1m29s).
