## Readonly and frozen component disposal (2026-10-06)

The previous mapper assigned to every original `dispose` property, rejecting legal readonly, getter-backed and frozen component objects during mount. `.local/component-disposal-before.log` retains 11 failing behavioral scenarios (13 failures including parent test groups). `backend/component-disposal.ts` now tracks cleanup separately from component mutation. Writable/configurable data methods receive an instance-local guard with their existing descriptor flags; inherited readonly methods can be shadowed on extensible instances. Locked own methods, accessor descriptors and nonextensible objects retain their original property. Getter disposal methods are read only at cleanup, with the current original callback and receiver.

Original Pi custom UI calls the returned root's `dispose` and delegates descendant cleanup to that original parent (`interactive-mode.js`, custom UI close). The desktop follows that authority for descendants whose calls cannot be intercepted, avoiding an additional mapper call after the parent callback. A parent that deliberately handles only itself keeps that original behavior. A tree without a parent disposer can still release its tracked children. Replaceable methods retain their existing exactly-once guard and exception isolation. Observable immutable descendants still in use defer their original disposing owner tree until it can retire; their input and SDK state remain live during renderer replacement. This does not infer inaccessible private references or suppress explicit calls inside multiple immutable original disposers.

Two further ownership reproductions failed before the final correction (`.local/component-disposal-ownership-before.log`): permanent delegation skipped cleanup of a cached immutable child after reuse, and a getter that cleaned a child before throwing caused an additional child call. Delegation now lasts only for the current release pass. Descendants are claimed before resolving a disposal getter, and already-disposed tracked owners seed that pass. Only actual guarded cleanup enters the permanent disposed set. Cached children can remain alive after an original parent cleans only itself and receive their own cleanup on later retirement.

The final focused disposal suite passes 18/18 (`.local/component-disposal-source-final.log`). The full integration run passes 738/738 (`.local/component-disposal-all-tests-final-ownership.log`) and TypeScript checking passes (`.local/component-disposal-check-ownership.log`). The initial check failures were confined to test types: the public TUI type does not declare Container, and the renderer test omitted required context flags; the tests now use the original runtime constructor and complete context. The audit remains 458 declaration exports and 156 unchanged runtime exports (`.local/component-disposal-sdk-audit.log`).

The final release build passes, including TypeScript checking (`.local/component-disposal-native-build-final.log`; Rust compilation 1m30s). Native `--component-disposal` passes all 11 workflows against that executable with legacy presentations disabled (`.local/component-disposal-native-verified-disposal.log`). Its initial final-build attempt timed out before initialization and recorded no SDK actions (`.local/component-disposal-native-final-disposal.log`); the unchanged executable passed on the sequential rerun. No startup fix is claimed. Source and native checks preserve actual xterm input, original completion results, SDK changes and cleanup ownership.

The final affected browser run passed 178 cases and failed the two new cached-child cases (`.local/component-disposal-ui-final.log`). The failures were test cursor assumptions: Pi's `setValue("restored")` retained the previous cursor position, so typing produced `restore!d`. The workflow now explicitly uses the original line-end key before typing. Both unchanged product-code cases pass on rerun (`.local/component-disposal-cached-ui-rerun.log`). The first isolated rerun failed to start because its temporary configuration used `.local` as the server working directory; explicit project-root directories corrected that configuration. Together, the main run and corrected rerun establish 180 distinct passing affected cases, including 22 disposal cases, private-storage transitions, opaque descendants, delegated-render/collection cases, focus/listeners/cleanup/lifecycle, standard mapping/overlays/editors and all five actual xterm cases. This is not a single clean 180-case run or a fresh complete default browser suite. Desktop/narrow cached restoration and native cached/frozen/transition screenshots were inspected. Browser fixtures enable legacy examples; separate generic native workflows disable them.

Final native `--terminal-transition` passes all 17 preceding workflows, separate `--mapped-components` passes standard controls/editor transactions, and separate `--terminal --mapped-components` passes actual xterm/PTY, inherited synchronous child input/resize and original component callbacks (`.local/component-disposal-native-verified-transition.log`, `.local/component-disposal-native-verified-mapped.log`, `.local/component-disposal-native-verified-terminal.log`). Combined terminal flags select the terminal workflow, so standard mapping was tested separately. All three final native exit modes await one original asynchronous shutdown hook and exit cleanly (`.local/component-disposal-native-verified-shutdown-window.log`, `.local/component-disposal-native-verified-shutdown-sdk.log`, `.local/component-disposal-native-verified-shutdown-overlap.log`).

The final staged runtime passes six transport cases in `.local/component-disposal-staged-terminal-final.log`. Its PTY case exceeded the 15-second endpoint-startup wait with no stderr output. The unchanged isolated PTY rerun passes 1/1 (`.local/component-disposal-staged-terminal-final-retry.log`). These runs establish all seven covered behaviors, not a single clean seven-case run or a startup correction. The native terminal workflow also passes against the final executable.

Final source-bundle, staged-runtime and native-resource backend copies share SHA-256 `84E4F87EF113F060825BBF596C232394DDF895BB96ABF6EB15040530C47D73F9`. The tested executable has SHA-256 `896DE225BC30E0F73ED24AD117D04BD2D1C4B3389E75F5891384AAE41C012CC2`, recorded in `.local/component-disposal-tested-build.json`. Final NSIS packaging passes (`.local/component-disposal-bundle-final.log`). The 126,535,891-byte installer has SHA-256 `648EA0B14FF42FA180466216BAEA132F2B63AC1B5A0849542AE0AB8789BC2FF4`. The receipt helper verified that packaging retained the tested executable and backend identities, then recorded `.local/component-disposal-artifact-hashes.json`. Installed-copy behavior was not exercised.

The owned preview at `http://127.0.0.1:1450/` now runs the final source (backend wrapper PID 30460, backend listener PID 31236, UI PID 29752, backend port 4350). The initial refresh encountered an occupied backend port after graceful shutdown left the old listener alive. After verifying and stopping only the owned old preview processes, the final launch's listener ownership and proxy authentication identity were checked. HTTP 200, cached restoration, original input and final parent/child cleanup pass (`.local/component-disposal-preview-final-check.log`); the preview screenshot was inspected. Source/test and current document formatting checks pass.

The full SDK completion goal remains open. Remaining verification includes inaccessible/getter-backed render transitions, initial private ownership, shared native/terminal appearances, multiple original immutable disposer owners, reuse across separate mapper factories, arbitrary geometry, real external services, physical OS input, installed-copy behavior and future SDK upgrades. The scenarios above establish supported disposal contracts, not universal interception of arbitrary JavaScript calls.

## Desktop/xterm presentation transitions (2026-10-06)

Previously mapped children could lose focus and be prematurely disposed when an extension moved their references into closure, WeakMap or JavaScript private-field storage. `.local/terminal-transition-before.log` retains all 15 failing holder/type reproductions. `delegatedRender` now observes previously known instances as well as explicit data references, without invoking render getters. Actual original render calls establish current ownership. Changed-width output continues through xterm; returning to an observable same-width frame restores desktop controls with the same Pi instance, original callbacks, focus and SDK mutations. This does not discover arbitrary private references or patch Pi packages/prototypes.

Composite SDK focus also falls back to its own `data-component-action` element when no existing leaf/pointer action is present. The element receives `tabIndex=-1`, preserving the original composite keyboard owner. Readonly SettingsList and a custom Container verify this behavior; the initial SettingsList browser failure and its focused reproduction are retained in `.local/terminal-transition-ui-smoke.log` and `.local/terminal-transition-ui-settings.log`.

## Focused descendants in opaque terminal frames (2026-10-06)

Opaque terminal wrappers without a root `handleInput` could lose their original focused child during refresh and swallow keyboard input. The source reproductions are retained in `.local/terminal-descendant-before.log`, `.local/terminal-descendant-clear-before.log` and `.local/terminal-descendant-owner-before.log`. Nonempty terminal frames now register explicit descendants for input/lifetime, project their focus onto the visible xterm and send raw terminal data through the original Pi keyboard path. A frame without a handler routes to Pi's current focus; an existing wrapper handler keeps priority without replacing that focus. Listeners can redirect, consume or clear focus during the current key. Explicit cleared focus also prevents later bare input from implicitly selecting a control. A closure-held unregistered target can project when its surface has a single terminal frame.

The focused source suite passes 23/23 (`.local/terminal-descendant-source-final.log`), and the full integration suite passes 704/704 (`.local/terminal-descendant-all-tests.log`). Final TypeScript checking passes (`.local/terminal-descendant-check-final.log`). The affected browser suite passes 90/90 in 8.8 minutes (`.local/terminal-descendant-related-ui.log`), including 44 new desktop/narrow cases, 41 existing native-component regressions and five actual xterm cases. The new cases cover Input, Editor, CustomEditor, SelectList and SettingsList under multiple child renders, changed widths, nonconfigurable render methods and closure-held ownership, plus wrapper handler priority and multiple opaque regions. Actual xterm textarea focus, clicking an emitter, Pi Ctrl+A line-start semantics, bracketed paste, synthetic composition, same-key listener redirection, original results, clear/restore and exactly-once disposal pass. The initial two browser failures were test expectations: a request used `id` instead of `surfaceId`, and unchanged Pi focus was expected to reassert DOM focus after clicking another emitter. The corrected tests preserve the original global focus contract; no frontend correction was needed.

Native `--terminal-descendant` passes all 22 workflows; separate native `--mapped-components` and `--terminal --mapped-components` runs pass against the fresh executable with legacy presentations disabled (`.local/terminal-descendant-native.log`, `.local/terminal-descendant-native-mapped.log`, `.local/terminal-descendant-native-terminal.log`). Combined terminal flags select the terminal workflow, so standard mappings run separately. Desktop/narrow and native terminal screenshots were inspected. The browser preview fixture enables legacy examples; generic production/native workflows disable them. The preceding complete 455-case browser run remains historical evidence and was not repeated after this routing change.

The staged runtime passes all seven transport/terminal cases sequentially (`.local/terminal-descendant-staged-terminal-final.log`). Source integration tests also retain terminal and transport coverage. Each waits for one original asynchronous `session_shutdown` hook and exits cleanly. An installed-copy workflow was not repeated.

The executable has SHA-256 `92E1E9DCD51CDC5B36DDC332E166425DB1BF3EC87082F4D03376B44C6DCB21EE`, recorded in `.local/terminal-descendant-tested-build.json`. The same discovery runs after the original render to retain previously observed children still held through those collections when rendering skips them. ## Delegated component frames (2026-10-06)

Unknown wrappers can now retain standard desktop controls when their original render makes one observable child call at the same width, including wrappers adding captions, changing child rows or returning an empty frame. `component-delegation.ts` records the original wrapper output and a snapshot of each outer child call before in-place wrapper changes. Scoped probes restore original descriptors and preserve render replacements made by callbacks. Multiple calls, different widths and inaccessible/frozen render methods retain the original generic terminal fallback. No extension name, source hash, Pi fork or global prototype patch selects this path.

The mapper composes that original delegated frame around the child's existing desktop representation. Recursive row metadata survives transparent and decorated wrappers, so enclosing Box transformations keep their original input/list owner and unaffected native siblings. Input remains with the original wrapper handler. Explicit focus requests follow the input-owner chain back to the correct surface. Observed child instances are included in lifetime retention after mapping; previously observed children still directly held by the wrapper remain live when a render skips them. Empty unknown output becomes zero-height desktop content. Actual replacement releases the old instance exactly once; terminal wrappers also retain and dispose all observed children. Hidden SDK edits remain available and appear on restoration.

The first source reproduction fails before the mapping correction (`.local/delegated-frame-before.log`). A strengthened focus assertion reproduces 20 nested focus failures plus their parent (`.local/delegated-frame-focus-before.log`), and an early-return empty-frame probe reproduces the hidden path (`.local/delegated-frame-empty-before.log`). The final focused suite passes 38/38 (`.local/delegated-frame-source-final.log`): 30 combinations of five interactive types, one/three wrapper layers and decorated/transparent/nested shapes, replacement and multi-child terminal lifetime cases, their parent, and five delegation helper checks. Assertions cover original handler order, native values, masking, sibling preservation, focus, submission/selection/change, hidden SDK updates, restoration, descriptor preservation and exactly-once disposal. The final complete integration suite passes 673/673 (`.local/delegated-frame-all-tests-final.log`).

The final selected browser run passes 72/72 (`.local/delegated-frame-related-ui-final.log`), including 30 new desktop/narrow wrapper cases, authority/focus, cleanup/lifecycle, original transparent delegation, standard component/mouse/editor regressions and all five actual xterm cases. A separate recursive run passes 36/36 (`.local/delegated-frame-recursive-ui.log`), establishing 108 distinct affected browser cases. Earlier browser probes time out because the test continued targeting a raw input after an SDK setter or original Editor submission restored the native control (`.local/delegated-frame-ui-probe.log`, `.local/delegated-frame-ui-probe-fixed.log`). Corrected observation follows the current original presentation; the intermediate matrix then passes 30/30 (`.local/delegated-frame-ui-matrix.log`). ## Recursive vertical frame ownership (2026-10-05)

Parent-transformed frames now preserve original input/list ownership through nested Container/Box/VStack occurrences, including unchanged standard containers and containers with their own custom render additions. Each occurrence retains child ranges in its actual original frame; parent partial ranges retain both source and destination positions. Descendant comparisons therefore reuse the enclosing frame's existing padding/style and recurse through the same original component identities. Native unaffected inner and outer siblings remain mounted. Canonical frame capture applies to standard descendants only beneath compared ancestors. Weak internal range metadata does not cross the desktop transport. Pi 1.0.0 remains unchanged; no extension identity or source signature selects this behavior.

The initial nested source matrix reproduces lost input/list ownership (`.local/recursive-frame-before.log`). ## Nested interactive child frames (2026-10-05)

Custom Container/Box/VStack frames now retain the original Input/Editor/CustomEditor/SelectList/SettingsList input identity when their child rows are transformed, partly removed or receive internal insertions. Canonical child ranges and the original frame diff assign changed runs only when they have one original owner. Complete surviving children retain their native controls; an associated changed interactive child uses styled desktop lines and its original input sink. Stale native values/options are not mounted. Original handlers, setters, selection, search, submission and disposal remain on the original SDK objects. Native restoration retains current SDK state and unaffected sibling control identities. This is a component rule, independent of extension names and source signatures.

The initial nine-input matrix reproduces missing input ownership (`.local/nested-frame-before.log`). The component-render suite passes 74/74 (`.local/nested-frame-component-tests-final.log`), including all three containers with all five interactive types and stronger ANSI-aware removed-row assertions. The complete integration suite passes 288/288 (`.local/nested-frame-all-tests-final.log`); the later component-only run strengthens the removed-row assertion without changing the product. The SDK audit retains 458 declaration exports and 156 unchanged runtime exports (`.local/nested-frame-sdk-audit.log`). The fresh release build passes (`.local/nested-frame-native-build.log`). Pi remains unchanged and pinned to 1.0.0.

Mouse-triggered synchronous rendering exposed a canonical receiver copying the original temporarily scoped mouseLayout accessor. The independent receiver now supplies its own writable layout, without reading that scope or invoking its original setter. The targeted source regression fails before and passes after this change (`.local/nested-frame-layout-before.log`, `.local/nested-frame-layout-after.log`). Custom-frame pointer focus also suppresses the browser's default focus on the modal ancestor while retaining SDK pointer dispatch and ordinary link/button behavior. All 30 desktop/narrow browser matrix cases pass (`.local/nested-frame-ui-filter.log`); native `--nested-frame` passes the 15 container/type combinations against the fresh executable with legacy presentations disabled (`.local/nested-frame-native-final.log`). Native `--mapped-components` also passes (`.local/nested-frame-native-mapped.log`). Earlier attempts are not completion evidence. Test observation fixes exclude the raw sink from native-control locators, recognize native listboxes as well as dropdowns, publish direct list mouse callbacks and refresh SettingsList's original filter before programmatic selection. ANSI-aware fixture row removal follows Pi's original parser so cursor/style escapes cannot conceal the target text. Desktop/narrow and native matrix screenshots were inspected.

## Custom interactive input frames (2026-10-05)

Input, Editor and CustomEditor overrides that replace or transform their editable body now display the original styled frame through desktop DOM components. The stale native input/textarea is not mounted, and the input sink never receives the original SDK value. Keyboard, bracketed paste and composition commits still reach the original component handler after the original input listeners. These events carry no native text, selection or layout that could overwrite SDK state; original Pi bindings apply to this frame, including Ctrl+A line-start behavior. Canonical editor border row indices keep border-only helper changes on the existing native textarea path.

The shared focus bridge prioritizes the actual desktop action target over its outer mouse region. Switching between custom and native presentations retains the focused component. Original SDK setters, submission, mouse callbacks and exactly-once disposal remain live. Input buffers clear after the latest composition settles, preserving consecutive commits. Pi 1.0.0 remains unmodified and pinned; this is a generic component rule with no per-extension adapter or xterm fallback for these standard controls.

## Original SDK text and caret authority (2026-10-05)

Input, Editor and CustomEditor now publish versions of their original SDK text/caret state. Stale versioned keyboard requests discard obsolete browser text, selection and layout, then deliver the original key against the current SDK state. Stale selection and whole-value actions are rejected. The frontend tracks the version actually applied to its DOM control. Consecutive composition commits rebase onto the confirmed SDK caret; an older confirmation cannot replace a newer already-applied SDK value.

Version ownership is per control. Accepted ordinary edits keep their source control's generation, preserving queued input and asynchronous native selection. Callback changes to a sibling retire that sibling's previous context. A handler that changes its source and throws also retires the previous generation because its response failed. The original Input/Editor/CustomEditor handlers and classes remain unchanged. Failure evidence includes `.local/component-authority-exception-before.log`, `.local/component-authority-ownership-before.log`, `.local/component-authority-ui-selection.log` and `.local/committed-input-before.log`.

The complete integration suite passes 248/248 (`.local/component-authority-ownership-all-tests.log`). The focused authority matrix passes 14/14 (`.local/component-authority-ownership-after.log`), including all three text-control types with and without listeners, and sibling/failure callbacks through keyboard, submission and mouse. TypeScript and source formatting pass (`.local/component-authority-ownership-check-final.log`, `.local/component-authority-ownership-source-format.log`). The fresh SDK identity audit retains 458 declaration exports and 156 unchanged runtime exports (`.local/component-authority-ownership-sdk-audit.log`); Pi remains pinned to 1.0.0 without a fork or installed-package edits.

All 20 related browser cases pass (`.local/component-authority-ownership-ui.log`), covering control authority, focus, pointer replacement, selection confirmations/shortcuts, multiline input and actual xterm focus/pointers/palette/cursor/PTY operations. The six authority cases were subsequently expanded with exception recovery and rerun successfully at 1440px/390px (`.local/component-authority-exception-ui-verified.log`); these are six repeated cases, not 26 distinct tests. Desktop and narrow screenshots were inspected.

The release build passes (`.local/component-authority-ownership-native-build.log`). Native `--component-authority` passes the expanded original Input/Editor/CustomEditor workflows (`.local/component-authority-ownership-native-verified.log`). Its initial attempt could not install a wrapper on Tauri's read-only invoke property (`.local/component-authority-ownership-native.log`); the fixture now intercepts the actual fetch transport and asserts installation/hit counts. Subsequent selection setup skipped a select event while the preceding fill was awaiting confirmation (`.local/component-authority-native-selection-diagnosis.log`); the fixture now waits for the original SDK value and pending-input acknowledgement before selecting. These are test corrections, not additional product fixes. The separate HTTP exception fixture initially used `id` instead of `surfaceId` and was corrected before the passing browser run (`.local/component-authority-exception-ui.log`).

Native `--mapped-components` passes original standard controls, editor transactions, paste/composition, authentication fixtures, themes and window state (`.local/component-authority-ownership-native-mapped.log`). Native `--terminal --mapped-components` passes the terminal workflow with legacy example presentations disabled (`.local/component-authority-ownership-native-terminal.log`), including actual xterm focus/pointers/cursor/palette, synchronous inherited PTY child input and resize. Current native authority, mapped-control and xterm screenshots were inspected. Full browser, native shutdown and legacy native suites were not repeated for this update; previous package evidence remains below. No installed-copy test is claimed.

## Empty standard render frames and original SDK caret updates (2026-10-05)

All standard component overrides returning `[]` now suppress their native presentation, including inputs, editors, lists and containers. The mapped tree and original objects remain alive; rendering can be restored without disposing the hidden component. VisualLinePreview already displays its original frame and consumes empty output directly. This is a generic component rule, with no extension-specific selection or xterm fallback for standard empty frames.

## Passive standard render replacements (2026-10-05)

`Text`, `TruncatedText`, `Spacer` and `DynamicBorder` subclasses now retain replacements, removals and interior inserted lines from their original `render()` output. If the original standard body remains contiguous, the existing native component and its added headings/footers remain. Otherwise, the original frame replaces the passive component's presentation using structured desktop text; no terminal fallback or extension-specific recognition selects this path. Empty frames display no stale source text, spacing or border. Restoring standard rendering restores its semantic desktop presentation.

These four passive baselines render against a detached object with the original instance's own descriptors and the standard prototype. Pi's original invalidator clears only that detached cache before the standard render. The original instance, renderer, input/mouse callbacks and disposal remain owned by the component bridge. This prevents an original subclass's in-place `cachedLines` mutation from contaminating later baseline comparisons. Interactive/container baselines still use their existing layout behavior; this change does not establish arbitrary cache compatibility for every component.

Rendered frames are parsed as complete ANSI/OSC 8 text before splitting into desktop lines. Colors and links can therefore cross line boundaries, while empty/trailing rows retain line height. Unstyled runs normalize to plain text so added ANSI headings do not falsely replace unchanged native content.

The new four-type matrix first failed on every type (`.local/render-replacement-before.log`). All 30 component-render cases pass after implementation (`.local/render-replacement-fixed.log`), covering replacement/removal/restoration, cross-line colors, the original Text cache identity, unchanged render method, component identity and exactly-once disposal. All 223 integration tests and TypeScript pass (`.local/render-replacement-all-tests.log`, `.local/render-replacement-check.log`). The fresh audit retains all 458 declaration exports and 156 unchanged native runtime exports (`.local/render-replacement-sdk-audit.log`).

The initial browser attempt timed out during server startup before any test ran (`.local/render-replacement-ui.log`); the unchanged sequential run started successfully. That run passed 19/21 cases, including existing styles/Markdown, cleanup, appearance, additions and terminal workflows. Both new cases reached their callback check but their fixture handled only `click`, leaving Pi without a handled press from which to synthesize that click. The fixture now handles press/release without changing product dispatch. All six final render browser cases pass at desktop/narrow widths (`.local/render-replacement-ui-rerun.log`, `.local/render-replacement-final-ui.log`); initial failure artifacts remain in `.local/render-replacement-ui-failed`. New cases verify displayed replacement content, cross-line color/link state, blank/trailing row heights, no stale content on removal, native restoration, original pointer callback/result, unchanged focused sibling input and exactly-once cleanup of six components. Desktop/narrow screenshots were inspected.

The release build passes (`.local/render-replacement-native-build.log`). Native WebView2 `--render-additions` passes the new passive workflow, nested additions and the unchanged official modal editor with legacy presentations disabled (`.local/render-replacement-native.log`). ## DOM ScrollView layout before original input (2026-10-05)

The native scrollbar failure below exposed original input reaching Pi before updated DOM dimensions. A deterministic browser regression reproduced that behavior with ScrollView ResizeObserver delivery suspended: content grows from one to 60 rows, then scrollbar activation still uses the acknowledged `1:1` dimensions (`.local/layout-order-before.log`).

Mapped actions, keyboard/text/composition input and pointer callbacks now synchronize visible DOM scroll dimensions before original handlers run. ResizeObserver uses the same measurement path. Identical measurements join pending replies; changed measurements follow earlier reports, and nested scroll areas synchronize first. Hidden, inert and retired controls are skipped, with instance and pause/resume epochs guarding reused DOM elements. A response-gated browser scenario proves that the original callback waits for the in-flight acknowledgement and only one identical report is sent.

All 196 integration tests pass (`.local/layout-order-all-tests.log`). The final 15-case focused browser run passes seven layout scenarios plus focus, pause/resume, subclass additions and scrollbars at desktop/narrow widths (`.local/layout-order-final-ui.log`). Legacy example presentations are disabled. TypeScript, the release build and source formatting pass (`.local/layout-order-native-build.log`, `.local/layout-order-format.log`).

Native WebView2 action/keyboard/mouse scenarios pass with observer delivery suspended (`.local/layout-order-native-rerun.log`). The initial native attempt timed out waiting for the composer before initialization (`.local/layout-order-native.log`); an unchanged sequential rerun passed. No startup cause or product fix is claimed. Desktop/narrow and native layout screenshots were inspected. This resolves the reproduced layout/input ordering boundary; other standard-type render overrides, inherited helper overrides, interior geometry and per-character editable styling remain completion work.

## Terminal colors and cursor placement (2026-10-05)

Factory `queryTerminalColors` now queries actual xterm colors through a dedicated probe channel and original Pi parsers. It preserves partial timeouts, late callbacks, concurrent query isolation and owner cleanup. Pending probes replay when a renderer connects. Renderer writes are serialized so desktop probes cannot consume PTY program replies or keyboard input. Queries do not activate the terminal panel. Render-only components translate Pi cursor markers with original visible-width measurement and hardware-cursor settings; original render arrays are not mutated.

## xterm.js and real PTY support (2026-10-05)

The user explicitly selected xterm.js for terminal support, resolving the earlier architectural blocker. Standard Pi component types still map to desktop controls. Render-only component instances use a generic xterm fallback with their original input and completion callbacks. Terminal-dependent extensions run unchanged in a real PTY owned by a supervisor outside the SDK event loop. The worker protocol stays on a separate authenticated channel; terminal input and resize work while an original extension blocks in `spawnSync`.

The desktop command action now emits `user_bash`, preserving an extension result or its custom operations and recording the original result. The installed official interactive-shell example was copied unchanged into the test environment and completed interactive input with exit code 9. Tests also cover actual TTY flags, dimensions, inherited child input, Ctrl+C exit 130, idle Ctrl+C survival, working directory, original raw terminal APIs and protocol isolation. Existing SDK integration tests pass 186/186 (`.local/xterm-final-tests.log`); the final terminal/transport subset passes 7/7 (`.local/xterm-final-terminal-tests.log`).

The broad browser run passed 115/118 (`.local/xterm-all-ui.log`). Two failures were obsolete assertions banning xterm anywhere on the page; they now check that standard mapped controls remain desktop controls. One official-workflow timing failure passed on rerun. Subsequent testing exposed ordinary startup output opening the panel and covering controls; activation now follows extension terminal ownership instead of ordinary logs. All six focused standard-mapping, lifecycle, official-workflow and terminal cases pass (`.local/xterm-focused-ui.log`). Real pointer testing exposed an extension modal backdrop blocking the external terminal panel; its persistent DOM host now moves inside the extension modal focus scope. The three affected terminal/lifecycle cases pass with actual mouse clicks (`.local/xterm-pointer-fixed-ui.log`). This is combined regression evidence, not a claimed single clean 118-case run.

The SDK worker's process directory follows the validated workspace, including inherited child programs. Native terminal subscriptions do not wait for SDK dialog replay. A worker SIGINT handler prevents an idle terminal interrupt from killing the SDK. Windows runtime staging includes node-pty and its shipped native prebuilds. See [terminal architecture and remaining limits](terminal-compatibility.md).

### Final native channel correction and verification

The first packaged native run stalled before initialization (`.local/xterm-native-terminal.log`). The new supervisor had allowed an incoming native request ID to overwrite its own correlation ID. A regression with non-sequential string request IDs reproduced the timeout (`.local/xterm-request-id-before.log`). The supervisor now assigns its internal ID after copying the incoming request. All seven terminal/transport tests pass with the corrected IDs (`.local/xterm-request-id-fixed.log`).

The corrected bundled runtime passes actual WebView2 terminal mouse/keyboard interaction, synchronous child input/resize and original component completion (`.local/xterm-native-terminal-fixed.log`). Native window close, SDK-requested shutdown and overlapping shutdown each preserve exactly one original cleanup hook, including the 4.5-second window-close hook (`.local/xterm-native-sdk-shutdown-window.log`, `.local/xterm-native-sdk-shutdown.log`, `.local/xterm-native-sdk-shutdown-overlap.log`). ## Historical pre-xterm browser regression and completion review

The fresh full browser suite passes all 117 cases in 8.4 minutes (`.local/sdk-completion-current-browser.log`). This includes the recent lifecycle, input listeners, focus, shutdown, themes, geometry and editor cases alongside authentication fixtures, official workflows and standard dialogs. [Completion review](sdk-completion-review.md) now maps the user's full requirement to current evidence and explicit missing evidence. A source check confirmed Pi's SGR 21 bold-reset behavior is preserved; it did not justify changing the mapper to another terminal's double-underline convention. This was the pre-xterm boundary. The later explicit xterm.js instruction resolves that decision; see the current terminal implementation record above.

## Native protocol isolated from raw extension and inherited child output

A real extension using `fs.writeSync(1, ...)` and `spawnSync(..., { stdio: "inherit" })` reproduced non-protocol lines in the former native stdout transport (`.local/native-channel-before.log`). JavaScript stdout redirection cannot isolate those file descriptors. The native shell now owns a dedicated loopback listener with a random per-launch token; the backend connects using `--desktop-channel`, authenticates, and removes bootstrap credentials before loading extensions. Stdin is null, stdout/stderr are drained to the app log, and only the authenticated connection is parsed as desktop protocol. This is generic transport isolation and does not rewrite extensions or supply an interactive terminal. Legacy `--stdio` remains separately available with its original raw-output limitations.

The first connection tests exposed Windows connection resets on abrupt process exit (`.local/native-channel-tests.log`); the backend now ends the channel after joined lifecycle responses/events are queued. All 185 integration tests pass (`.local/native-channel-all-tests.log`), including three native-channel and three legacy-stdio shutdown paths. Native-channel fixtures include raw bytes, inherited-child output, unterminated output and a JSON shutdown event printed as ordinary output; none is parsed as a desktop event.

The first packaged native attempt timed out at initialization (`.local/native-channel-window.log`). A separate Rust regression reproduced `WouldBlock` after authentication on a socket accepted by a nonblocking Windows listener (`.local/native-channel-idle-before.log`). Accepted connections are now explicitly switched to blocking mode before authentication and background reading. Both Rust tests pass (`.local/native-channel-rust-final.log`), including a delayed packet after the handshake. This diagnoses this new-channel failure, not earlier unrelated startup timeouts.

Final packaging completed (`.local/native-channel-final-package.log`) with TypeScript and formatting checks. All three sequential final native paths pass with the raw-output extension and legacy adapters disabled: normal close with a completed 4.5-second hook (`.local/native-channel-window-final.log`), SDK shutdown (`.local/native-channel-sdk-final.log`), and reentrant shutdown (`.local/native-channel-overlap-final.log`). All verify one completed hook and exit code zero. Browser behavior did not change; its suite was not repeated. Full SDK support remains incomplete, and the independent-terminal choice is still pending.

All three backend copies share SHA-256 `08C1D46AA60648EC12F275508047A8660C020018DDF1ACBC576396CB9416245F`; final executable SHA-256 is `1064C1EB6AC8C8D4D46CD6C699A21BEE1C4B6EDFC7E808C482C71924325F1961`; final installer SHA-256 is `7EDE9FDB48D21433249642886BD6AE9C0F00607546DC7FF582C6AEFB56CCB297`.

## Pause gates desktop selection synchronization

A regression reproduced a paused original CustomEditor's caret moving from column 14 to column 0 despite its input handler consuming the event (`.local/component-paused-caret-before.log`). Registry input synchronization applied the event's selection before consulting the mapped handler. Mapped root/overlay controllers now expose their current input availability, and the registry checks it inside the queued operation before selection synchronization, action validation or mouse dispatch. Paused input is consumed without changing original state. Programmatic SDK editor changes remain allowed; the regression checks `setEditorText`/`getEditorText` while paused and preservation after resume.

All 182 integration cases pass (`.local/component-paused-caret-tests.log`). Four wide/narrow browser lifecycle and programmatic-focus cases pass (`.local/component-paused-caret-browser.log`). Complete SDK behavioral support remains unproven.

## Mapped TUI desktop pause and resume

The new regression reproduced the absence of desktop pause semantics after original factory `tui.stop()` (`.local/component-tui-lifecycle-before.log`). Mapped instances now implement desktop pause/resume without calling terminal lifecycle functions or modifying Pi prototypes. Pause freezes the last mapped view, makes it inert, releases capture and gates input, actions, mouse and appearance delivery. Resume invalidates original components and presents current state. Overlays retain paused views/heights and renderer source updates defer until resume. Retired factories cannot restart their surface.

The first browser run found a separate editor-response leak: a submission callback stopped the surface and changed the original input, and the pending input response displayed the changed value despite the frozen snapshot (`.local/component-tui-lifecycle-browser.log`). Paused input/mouse responses now suppress editor updates. The expanded integration regression covers this path. All 179 integration tests pass (`.local/component-tui-lifecycle-final-tests.log`); all four wide/narrow browser lifecycle and existing invalidation cases pass (`.local/component-tui-lifecycle-final-browser.log`). Browser checks verify that an inert input cannot acquire focus and that resume retains the exact DOM input instance. TypeScript, formatting and web/backend build pass (`.local/component-tui-lifecycle-build.log`).

The owned preview was refreshed on port 1429/backend 4328, supervisor PID 24164. This source/web/backend update has not yet been staged into the packaged native application or installer; the preceding native-window-close package remains the latest native artifact. Full SDK support remains incomplete, including terminal ownership and arbitrary render-only presentations. Overlay lifecycle and physical OS input need further direct verification; the tests above do not prove those behaviors.

## Native window close awaits original asynchronous cleanup

The packaged window-close regression reproduced truncation of an original `session_shutdown` hook: its `started` marker was present, but its completion marker after 4.5 seconds was missing (`.local/sdk-shutdown-window-repro.log`). The native shell previously allowed only three seconds before killing the backend. Normal window exit now defers app termination, sends one shutdown request and waits on a background thread for backend completion. Duplicate window exit requests share this path. A broken input pipe terminates/reaps the child and reports a nonzero exit. Successful explicit shutdown responses are recognized as intentional backend closure.

TypeScript checking and Rust formatting/tests pass (`.local/sdk-shutdown-window-rust.log`). Packaging completed (`.local/sdk-shutdown-window-package.log`). Three sequential tests against the final packaged executable pass with legacy adapters disabled: normal window close with a 4.5-second hook (`.local/sdk-shutdown-window-native.log`), extension context shutdown (`.local/sdk-shutdown-window-native-sdk.log`), and shutdown requested again inside the original cleanup hook (`.local/sdk-shutdown-window-native-overlap.log`). Each checks completed original cleanup exactly once and desktop exit code zero before harness cleanup. This change does not impose a timeout on normal cleanup; a hook that never settles can keep the background process alive. Full SDK behavioral compatibility remains incomplete. The browser and integration suites were not repeated for this native lifecycle change.

All three backend copies retain SHA-256 `1E3B10BDDDFCE4561F9719D5C6DE929298A4A6E9A4B90087491ABDFAA00E7BC6`; executable SHA-256 is `6BD7290B778B256B92DC8515535A865CF22710F301DE2032A4F35DDD42D42BCD`; installer SHA-256 is `4EF740A235974DBE4A90D9C5447DFA55F878B5CB00E6F1E74BD13A05BD4557EF`.

## Concurrent and reentrant SDK shutdown

A controlled runtime-disposal barrier reproduced two original `dispose()` calls when SDK shutdown and window cleanup overlapped (`.local/sdk-shutdown-concurrent-before.log`). Host disposal now publishes a shared promise before starting eager cancellation. Concurrent and reentrant callers receive the same completion/error; subsequent calls do not rerun original hooks. Direct initialization and new actions reject after disposal has begun, rather than attempting to reuse the retired host.

A second process regression reproduced a lost graceful shutdown event when a `session_shutdown` hook requested SDK shutdown during a transport-initiated close (`.local/sdk-shutdown-flush-before.log`). Stdio exit now waits for all callbacks joining that disposal to publish their packets, then flushes the protocol pipe. Explicit cleanup errors return an error response and nonzero exit instead of an unhandled rejection/success acknowledgement. The six focused lifecycle/transport cases pass (`.local/sdk-shutdown-concurrent-final.log`), followed by the full 177-test suite (`.local/sdk-shutdown-concurrent-tests.log`). An additional cleanup-error regression passes separately (`.local/sdk-shutdown-concurrent-error.log`), checking identical original errors and a single underlying disposal; there are now 178 tests in the suite. TypeScript and formatting pass. Both final packaged native paths pass with legacy adapters disabled: `--sdk-shutdown-overlap` (`.local/sdk-shutdown-concurrent-native-overlap.log`) and `--sdk-shutdown` (`.local/sdk-shutdown-concurrent-native.log`). Each verifies one completed original shutdown hook and native exit code zero before harness cleanup. Neither attempt reproduced the prior initialization timeout. Full SDK behavioral support remains incomplete; browser behavior did not change and its suite/export audit were not repeated for this backend lifecycle fix.

Packaging completed (`.local/sdk-shutdown-concurrent-package.log`). All three backend copies share SHA-256 `1E3B10BDDDFCE4561F9719D5C6DE929298A4A6E9A4B90087491ABDFAA00E7BC6`; executable SHA-256 is `054A3B512BF33C6325DD0AF5D96996831889F13E66151E198EAE0E800267870E`; installer SHA-256 is `92674D7E098F6219CBCD1E553B58C6CC5281FF7D246129D63403E5F091458DA3`. ## SDK context shutdown lifecycle

An isolated transport regression confirmed that extension `ctx.shutdown()` disposed the SDK session but left its server process running (`.local/sdk-shutdown-before.log`). The host now admits one shutdown request from the current session, waits for original runtime disposal, then publishes a graceful shutdown event. Stdio flushes this final event before exiting. HTTP closes its clients/server. The native Rust shell treats that event as a normal app exit and suppresses the backend-crash notice. Browser subscriptions stop reconnecting and show a closed state; late snapshots cannot restore it. New desktop operations are rejected once shutdown starts.

All 175 integration tests pass (`.local/sdk-shutdown-tests.log`). Process tests cover both transports, duplicate requests, one completed asynchronous `session_shutdown` hook, final event delivery and exit code zero. The lifecycle regression also verifies that a stale context cannot shut down the replacement session, cleanup clears mounted surfaces, and a new session request during shutdown is rejected. Four existing default-editor browser cases pass (`.local/sdk-shutdown-browser.log`). The initial shutdown UI test withheld the event stream while waiting for initialization that depends on its connection; that test-precondition error was corrected, and the final browser shutdown-state case passes (`.local/sdk-shutdown-browser-final.log`). This browser test supplies a shutdown event; real server exit is separately verified by the process tests. TypeScript, Prettier and Rust formatting pass. Full SDK behavioral support remains incomplete.

The initial native attempt timed out before initialization, before sending the shutdown command (`.local/sdk-shutdown-native.log`). Its cause is unconfirmed; there was no new backend crash log. Both transport cases pass against the packaged Node/backend files (`.local/sdk-shutdown-packaged-transport.log`). A native startup probe received a normal backend response and the shutdown workflow passed (`.local/sdk-shutdown-native-probe.log`); after removing that temporary probe, the uninstrumented final native `--sdk-shutdown` also passes (`.local/sdk-shutdown-native-final.log`). It invokes the extension's original context twice and verifies one completed shutdown hook and desktop exit code zero before harness cleanup, with legacy adapters disabled. No product or package changes occurred between those native attempts.

Packaging completed (`.local/sdk-shutdown-package.log`). ## Listener focus with browser control context and function keys

A real browser-context regression showed an input listener selecting the second Input while the pending key still replaced the first Input's selection (`.local/component-listener-focus-before.log`). Explicit focus changes during listener dispatch now take precedence over the old browser control identifier. Source text/selection/layout context does not transfer to the destination, and the response reconciles the source rather than writing the destination's result into it. Clearing focus consumes the pending input without sending it to a control. Calls without a browser control identifier retain the destination's editor result.

The browser workflow then exposed incorrect plain F1–F4 encoding: Pi's installed parser does not recognize CSI `1;1P` through `1;1S`, but recognizes their SS3 forms. A separate failing parser regression confirms this (`.local/component-function-keys-before.log`). The bridge now uses SS3 for unmodified F1–F4. The parser regression verifies every plain F1–F12 key using both `matchesKey` and `parseKey`; this does not claim new support for modified function keys beyond Pi's own parser.

All 171 integration tests pass (`.local/component-listener-focus-final-tests.log`), and four desktop/narrow browser cases pass (`.local/component-listener-focus-final-browser.log`). The extended shared listener workflow selects the source input, presses F2, checks original destination focus, both independent values, and the original returned result. It also retains the Text-only listener/debug checks and existing focus regression. Formatting and the packaged build's TypeScript check pass. The final packaged native `--component-listeners` passes that extended shared workflow with legacy adapters disabled (`.local/component-listener-focus-native.log`). Full SDK behavioral support remains incomplete; the full browser suite and export audit were not repeated for this focused change.

Packaging completed (`.local/component-listener-focus-package.log`). ## Terminal ownership audit

Source inspection of the pinned Pi 1.0.0 `TuiBase.start/stop`, `TuiMainScreen`, the bundled `examples/extensions/interactive-shell.ts`, and `backend/detached-tui.ts` confirms a remaining terminal-ownership gap. Factory `tui.start()` invokes the unsupported detached terminal start operation. Native `tui.stop()` is still present; it can return without a terminal write when there are no rendered lines, but it does not suspend desktop surface input. Neither method provides desktop pause/resume semantics. The interactive-shell example additionally writes directly to stdout and starts a subprocess with inherited stdio, which component-type mapping cannot turn into desktop controls. No no-op substitute was added and no product code changed in this audit. Existing regression results below remain the latest executed evidence; this source audit does not establish full SDK compatibility.

Focus revisions remain monotonic, and a new explicit `tui.setFocus()` creates a new request; original hidden instances retain their values and lifetime.

## Original programmatic component focus

An ordinary factory calling `tui.setFocus(secondInput)` left the corresponding desktop input inactive at both browser widths (`.local/component-focus-before.log`). Explicit original TUI focus changes now produce a revisioned focus request on the owning desktop surface. The request carries the original component action identity and follows the actual focus decision made by Pi. Internal pointer/caret synchronization does not create another explicit focus request, and repeated identical focus calls do not repeatedly focus the DOM.

The frontend applies each request to a visible enabled control within the same surface instance, and supports clearing focus. Ordinary snapshots do not replay unchanged focus. A scheduled focus is cancelled on effect replacement/unmount or intervening pointer/key activity. Overlays retain their visibility/focus guards. ## SDK theme changes invalidate original component caches

A regression reproduced a stale original Text cache after `ui.setTheme("dark")`: the SDK theme changed but the component's invalidation callback was never invoked (`.local/theme-invalidate-before.log`). Registry invalidation revisions also cover factories that resolve after a theme change while they were pending.

Callbacks retain the original instances and theme proxy. Invalidation errors report independently, and registry identity is rechecked if a pending component closes itself during invalidation, preventing it from being remounted. A root directly registered as a native child or overlay is not invoked twice. Retired factories ignore invalidation calls.

## Asynchronous factory retirement and first-render cleanup

A delayed component factory reproduced rendering after its surface had already been replaced (`.local/component-late-factory-before.log`). The early abort had already run cleanup before the root existed; the returned root was subsequently rendered/tracked, but the second cleanup was skipped. A separate regression showed that a first-render exception left the root and child undisposed (`.local/component-first-render-before.log`).

The generic mapper now takes ownership of the returned standard component tree before running original render/editor setup hooks. If its factory lifetime has already ended, it releases the late tree without rendering or configuring it; the registry discards the inactive result without affecting a successor. Original parent/child disposers still run once, including when the parent disposes its child. Tests cover replacement, explicit close and registry disposal while the factory waits, plus first-render exceptions and the existing asynchronous nested-overlay input workflow (`.local/component-factory-lifetime-after.log`).

## Component TUI color queries and appearance notifications

An ordinary standard-component factory failed when awaiting `tui.queryTerminalColors()` or enabling `setTerminalColorSchemeNotifications(true)`: both still reached terminal writes in the native TUI implementation. Two focused regressions reproduce these failures (`.local/component-appearance-before.log`). Detached TUI color queries now resolve immediately with `{}`, using Pi's contract for absent terminal-reported colors; this does not invent a palette or reinterpret application theme tokens as terminal replies. There is no terminal reply stream or late-reply callback to schedule.

Mapped factories receive actual desktop appearance changes through `onTerminalColorSchemeChange`. Notifications honor enable/disable, duplicate appearance reports do not emit again, and unsubscribe removes the original callback. Each factory owns its subscription through its lifetime signal, so close, replacement, failure and disposal release it. Synchronous `renderNow` also uses the desktop invalidation path for all detached TUI consumers. Original component factories, callbacks and instances remain in use, without extension-specific adapters or installed Pi changes.

## Standard editor submission rules

Standard multi-line dialog submit/newline inputs now run through the original Pi Editor handler, including configured keys and its legacy newline encodings. This retains Pi's backslash/Enter workaround under both default keys and a Shift+Enter submit/Enter newline configuration, and returns the original trimmed submission value. Non-submitting line changes replace the desktop's selected range in one native text transaction. Explicit desktop dialog answers also trim multi-line editor strings so the confirmation button has the same returned-value semantics; single-line input and selection values retain their original treatment. Cancellation and external-editor wrapper handling still precede the editor handler.

The regression failed before implementation (`.local/dialog-submit-before.log`) and now passes (`.local/dialog-submit-after.log`), checking default/remapped submission, first-line and internal whitespace preservation, backslash cursor behavior, selected newline replacement and confirmation-button answer routing. Existing large-paste browser expectations now distinguish full editable content from Pi's trimmed submitted answer. TypeScript and formatting pass. The shared submission workflow is wired into native `--dialog-reconnect`, but native packaging/verification has not been repeated for this source update. The installer below predates this change. Full SDK behavioral compatibility remains unproven.

## Standard dialog paste streams

Standard text dialogs now retain original Pi Input/Editor paste state between input chunks. The initial desktop range is held until the native component finishes its paste; intermediate chunks leave the visible text and undo history intact. An end marker split across chunks is recognized by the original parser. Multi-line submit/newline keys enter that parser while a paste is pending, while cancellation/external-editor wrapper keys retain their precedence. Original single-line wrapper submit/cancel behavior remains active. If a multi-line paste has a trailing submit sequence, the original Editor's submission value resolves the dialog, including empty submitted strings. Dialog lifetime owns the controller, so closed dialogs reject late chunks.

The regression failed before implementation (`.local/dialog-stream-before.log`) and passes afterward (`.local/dialog-stream-after.log`). It covers both dialog kinds, split terminators, buffered carriage returns, trailing submission, cancellation and stale chunks. All 154 integration tests pass (`.local/dialog-stream-integration.log`). Both desktop/narrow paste browser cases pass (`.local/dialog-stream-browser.log`), including an actual extension input listener that transforms three keys into a paste stream, its original answer and one native undo/redo transaction. TypeScript and formatting pass. The shared workflow also retains the preceding complete-paste, normalization and expanded-large-content checks. Full SDK behavioral compatibility remains unproven.

All eight shared browser dialog cases pass (`.local/dialog-stream-final-browser.log`). The owned preview was refreshed at `http://127.0.0.1:1429/` (supervisor PID 9016, backend 4328), with HTTP 200. ## Standard dialog paste semantics

Standard text dialogs now capture desktop paste/beforeinput/composition through the existing input queue even when no extension input listener is registered. Complete bracketed paste goes through the original Pi Input or Editor after listeners/shortcuts. Single-line normalization removes line endings and expands tabs; multi-line normalization preserves normalized line breaks, filters control bytes and decodes Pi's supported CSI-u control-byte form. Browser selections are replaced once, and the native undo/redo transaction remains available. The multi-line command engine expands its own large-paste markers and caret prefix before returning the desktop text transaction, so the desktop shows actual content and submission returns it without leaking terminal presentation markers.

The regression failed before implementation (`.local/dialog-paste-before.log`). Both widths of the new browser paste workflow pass (`.local/dialog-paste-browser.log`), covering paste events, selected replacement, native undo/redo, large content and original submission values. This shared workflow is included in native `--dialog-reconnect`, but native packaging/verification has not been repeated for this source update. The installer recorded below predates this change. TypeScript and source formatting pass. Full SDK behavioral compatibility remains unproven; fragmented raw paste streams and real OS IME/mobile input remain outside this focused verification.

## Standard dialog editing commands

Standard input/editor dialogs now own a Pi command controller for their lifetime. Multi-line dialogs reuse the existing original Editor command engine; single-line dialogs use an original Input instance, preserving its word deletion, kill accumulation, yank/yank-pop and grapheme movement. Both paths honor configured keys after extension input listeners and shortcuts, preserve desktop selection deletion and native undo/redo, and return text/caret transactions through the existing ordered input queue. The single-line bridge scopes the keybinding manager to a synchronous call and restores it in `finally`; no installed source or global prototype is patched. Closed dialogs discard their command state, and late input cannot reach it. Read-only external-editing controls do not execute editing commands.

## Standard confirmation component keyboard mapping

Confirmation dialogs now use Pi's selector semantics: Yes is index 0, No is index 1, navigation uses the configured select keys, and confirmation returns the original boolean. Select/confirm dialogs also dispatch `app.tools.expand` to the original UI context without closing the dialog. The backend regression first failed before the fix (`.local/dialog-confirm-before.log`) and passes afterward (`.local/dialog-confirm-after.log`).

The desktop initializes focus on the first logical option or text field through a stable mount ref. This avoids depending on the modal's animation-completion callback, which did not establish focus in the browser regression, and avoids resetting user focus on ordinary rerenders. These are shared Pi dialog/component rules, not adapters for individual extensions.

All 151 integration tests pass (`.local/dialog-confirm-integration.log`), and all six shared browser cases pass at desktop/mobile widths (`.local/dialog-confirm-final-browser.log`), covering confirmation booleans, initial focus, navigation, tool expansion, text/option identity, external editing, deadlines and recovery. ## Standard dialog external editing

Standard multi-line dialogs now resolve `app.editor.external` through Pi's configured keybindings and invoke the existing desktop external-editor runner with their own draft and working directory. Each dialog owns its child operation; duplicate requests reuse that operation. Launching the editor does not block the keyboard queue, so cancellation remains available. The textarea becomes read-only until the editor settles, failures restore editing, and a result applies only to its still-mounted, unchanged control in the same session. The original SDK dialog promise remains pending until the user submits or cancels.

Standard dialog requests now combine their caller/authorization signal with the current session lifetime. Closing the dialog, resource reload and host disposal abort its child and discard stale results; disposal waits for temporary-file cleanup. Tests cover successful results, failures and retries, close/reload/dispose cancellation and cleanup, stale calls, and a remapped external-editor key. All 151 integration tests pass (`.local/dialog-external-all-tests.log`). The keyboard workflow is shared with native `--dialog-reconnect` QA. This does not establish arbitrary terminal-editor interaction or all SDK functionality.

## Standard dialog keyboard dispatch

A browser regression reproduced Enter leaving an SDK input dialog open (`.local/dialog-keyboard-before.log`). Standard dialog keys now enter the existing desktop input queue, run extension input listeners/shortcuts, and resolve dialog commands through Pi's configured keybinding manager. Input/editor submission, editor newline, selection navigation and cancellation preserve the existing dialog callback. Native links/action buttons retain their own activation; Escape cannot bypass a remapped cancellation key. Retired dialog requests ignore late key actions.

Queued option keys now use the focus established by earlier navigation within the same dialog. The backend integration case verifies remapped confirmation/navigation/cancellation/newline/submission, listener consumption/replacement and original results (`.local/dialog-input-integration.log`). All 150 integration tests pass (`.local/dialog-keyboard-integration.log`); TypeScript and formatting pass. Standard dialog editor external-editor invocation and the complete set of editing shortcuts remain outside this focused verification; full SDK support remains unproven. This source change is newer than the latest Windows package.

All six related browser cases pass (`.local/dialog-keyboard-final-browser.log`), including rapid selection/confirmation and multiline entry at both widths, plus styled options, links, deadlines and reconnect. The owned preview was refreshed at `http://127.0.0.1:1429/` (supervisor PID 11396, backend 4328), with HTTP 200. Native packaging and the export audit were not repeated for this source update.

## Native pending-dialog recovery

The HTTP event stream already replayed pending dialogs, but the native WebView event subscription did not. Reloading with an outstanding extension selector reproduced a missing dialog in the previous package (`.local/native-dialog-replay-before.log`). The native subscription now attaches its event listener before calling `dialog.list`, then reconciles dialog events received while the list response is pending. Closed requests are removed, newly opened requests retained, and duplicate IDs replayed once; unrelated events continue normally. Failed list reads release the listener. Backend requests retain their original callbacks, presentation and deadlines.

Both replay race/cleanup tests pass (`.local/dialog-replay-tests.log`), and all 149 integration tests pass (`.local/dialog-replay-integration.log`). Browser/native dialog checks now share `tests/dialog-workflows.ts`; native verification skips opening an external URL but still checks the actual link destination and separate option-selection callback. Full SDK compatibility remains unproven.

All four shared browser cases pass (`.local/dialog-replay-browser.log`). The fresh packaged `--dialog-reconnect` native workflow passes pending-dialog restoration, styled text and option identity, all three dialog timeout results, non-positive timeout input, and reload without deadline reset (`.local/native-dialog-replay-after.log`). The native screenshot was inspected. TypeScript and formatting pass. The owned preview was refreshed at `http://127.0.0.1:1429/` (supervisor PID 6220, backend 4328), with HTTP 200.

## Standard dialog text mapping

Standard extension/provider dialogs previously bypassed the component text mapper. A browser regression reproduced styled titles failing to appear as clean text (`.local/dialog-text-before.log`). Dialog requests now retain their raw title, message, placeholder and option values alongside structured presentation. Titles, messages, option labels/descriptions and styled input hints use the existing styled-text renderer. Input/editor accessible names use clean title text. Identically displayed options continue returning their distinct original strings.

Options containing links use separate link content and selection buttons, avoiding nested interactive controls and unintended selection when opening a link. The original option value remains the callback result. All 147 integration tests pass (`.local/dialog-text-integration.log`), including provider option IDs/descriptions and raw request preservation. Both initial browser viewport cases pass (`.local/dialog-text-after.log`). TypeScript and formatting pass. This source update and the preceding countdown change are newer than the latest Windows package; full SDK compatibility remains unproven.

The combined text/countdown browser run passes all four cases (`.local/dialog-text-final-browser.log`). Both final text cases also verify intercepted link opening keeps the dialog open and the independent selection button returns the original OSC-link string (`.local/dialog-text-link-selection.log`). The narrow screenshot was inspected. The owned preview was refreshed at `http://127.0.0.1:1429/` (supervisor PID 26372, backend 4328), with HTTP 200. The full browser suite, native package and export audit were not repeated for this source update.

## Dialog countdown and timeout semantics

The desktop host previously treated a negative dialog timeout as an immediate timer and omitted the SDK's live countdown. Pi 1.0.0 enables its dialog countdown only for positive values and rounds up to whole seconds. Regression tests reproduced the missing deadline and negative-timeout dismissal (`.local/dialog-timeout-before.log`). Positive finite timeouts now expose one backend deadline and expire on the rounded boundary; non-positive values remain untimed. The desktop title displays remaining seconds from that deadline. Answering or canceling clears the timer and late answers cannot complete the request twice.

All 146 integration tests pass (`.local/dialog-timeout-integration.log`), including mocked-time boundary, pre-aborted signal, cancellation and late-answer cases. Both browser widths pass real input/select/confirm countdown expiration, their original undefined/false results, and successful negative-timeout input (`.local/dialog-timeout-browser.log`). TypeScript passes. This source change is newer than the latest recorded Windows package; full SDK compatibility remains unproven.

The expanded browser workflow also passes at both widths after reloading with a pending dialog: the countdown resumes from its existing deadline and cancellation returns the original result (`.local/dialog-timeout-reconnect.log`). Formatting passes. The owned preview was refreshed at `http://127.0.0.1:1429/` (supervisor PID 17564, backend 4328), with HTTP 200. Native packaging and the export audit were not repeated for this source update.

## Hidden thinking label mapping

Pi's `setHiddenThinkingLabel` accepts styled text and preserves an explicit empty string. The desktop previously inserted the raw label into the summary and used truthiness to replace an empty label. The regression reproduced raw escape characters in summary text (`.local/thinking-label-before.log`). Labels now use the shared structured text mapper while the original SDK value remains in the snapshot; undefined restores the default and empty remains empty. The thinking block tracks its actual expanded state so manual expansion uses the expanded caption and collapsing restores the configured hidden label.

## Selection shortcut dispatch

A regression proved that native selection shortcuts returned before the original component handler ran (`.local/selection-shortcut-before.log`). Ctrl+A, selected Ctrl+C/Ctrl+X and Shift navigation now reach the original mapped handler. If it changes text or cursor, that result is retained; otherwise the existing desktop selection behavior runs. The native select-all convention still masks Pi's default Ctrl+A line-start binding during this call. Scoped keybindings restore afterward.

Exercising the original CustomEditor also reproduced a conflict with the host-installed Ctrl+C clear action (`.local/selection-shortcut-copy-before.log`). Host default clear/copy actions now defer when the editor has a selection, retaining desktop clipboard behavior. Extension-installed actions are preserved. Tests cover original Input and CustomEditor callbacks, text changes, error propagation and default selected copy/cut. A void handler that leaves state unchanged does not expose whether it intended to consume the key; this remains an inference limit of native fallback. The SDK's explicit terminal-input consume result remains available and runs before this path. No complete arbitrary-handler compatibility claim is made.

All 143 integration tests pass (`.local/selection-shortcut-final-tests.log`). Twelve related browser regressions passed during `.local/selection-shortcut-browser.log`; its new shortcut scenario initially failed because the test assumed `Input.setValue` moved the cursor to the end. Pi preserves the existing cursor, so the assertion was corrected from 16 to 14. Both focused viewport tests then passed (`.local/selection-shortcut-focused-browser.log`), followed by both actual selected-copy/cut cases using a page-local clipboard substitute (`.local/selection-shortcut-copy-browser.log`). This test does not modify the system clipboard. The selected browser suite was not rerun as one combined run after the assertion correction.

The packaged native `--editor-navigation` workflow passes (`.local/native-selection-shortcuts-final.log`), covering the original shortcut handlers, selected copy/cut, single-line and multiline mixed-direction navigation/deletion, visual rows and pending-input resize. The first native attempt timed out before initialization (`.local/native-selection-shortcuts.log`); its cause was not established. After packaging completed, a second attempt reached clipboard setup but failed because tsx inserted a function-name helper unavailable in the WebView (`.local/native-selection-shortcuts-retry.log`). The test-only injection now uses a self-contained JavaScript string; the final native run, TypeScript and formatting pass. These results do not establish all SDK behavior.

Packaging completed successfully (`.local/selection-shortcut-package.log`). All three backend copies share SHA-256 `B020F273C52119AE5C0D1CF5FA5544C3074C20923D74F33DA2D85823AE851EFC`; executable SHA-256 is `D3F2314B77AEE51E7B088123EC2F6BF101A8531BB33E09F9014E5A02998FF5BE`; installer SHA-256 is `211CFBBC0930F2D9E39896891603DDB069F0E1646AB99924885D977C956FD804`. ## Previous release: single-line Input navigation

The native-reference workflow reproduced the same selected-RTL-run collapse defect in standard single-line `Input`: expected offset 10, received 11 (`.local/input-navigation-before.log`). Input uses direct cursor writes rather than Editor's movement method, so the existing multiline bridge did not cover it.

Text-input controls now supply browser direction and unwrapped caret geometry. During an Input navigation operation, scoped keybindings apply RTL direction while the original handler still receives the original input data. Once Pi matches its movement command, scoped cursor/action fields supply range collapse without another character step. Original consuming handlers retain the range, and data-property descriptors are restored after successful handling or exceptions. This uses the pinned component implementation and no extension identity matching.

## Previous release: mixed-direction editor navigation
