## Previous release: mapped authorization presentation lifecycle

A real token-endpoint failure reproduced a stale desktop authorization banner in the original mapped MCP manager, while the slash-command path cleaned up correctly (`.local/mcp-failed-grant-before.log`). Pi catches this error inside its manager and returns to the menu without closing the custom UI scope or saving credentials. Its original error rendering and callback-listener cleanup already worked; credential-save completion alone could not observe this path.

Custom UI scopes now observe semantic links in their own mapped surface. Once the authorization URL has appeared, removing it ends that browser interaction without invoking the original component's cancel handler or aborting its UI lifetime. `auth_complete` dismisses the request and is not evidence of a successful login (cancellation already used this event). Pi continues token exchange and presents its actual result. No extension names, source hashes, error strings or terminal drawings are used to decide completion. URLs never presented as mapped links retain credential/scope-lifetime completion as a fallback.

The failure regression passes for slash and manager entry points (`.local/mcp-failed-grant-after.log`). All 20 authorization-focused tests and all 139 integration tests pass (`.local/mcp-presentation-lifecycle-tests.log`, `.local/mcp-presentation-final-tests.log`). A gated token response proves the browser request may close while the original token exchange is still pending; issuance and return to the original tools menu are checked separately. The earlier assertion that a tool name becomes unavailable after a rejected reconnect was invalid: Pi retains registered tool names. Tests now observe token issuance and original menu state directly. The intermediate failed test log is `.local/mcp-presentation-full-tests.log`.

Full SDK compatibility remains unproven. This change covers browser-request presentation lifetime; it does not infer success from UI removal or prove all background/cross-server authorization paths.

The packaged native `--mcp-oauth --mcp-identical-token --mcp-token-failure` workflow passes with legacy example presentations disabled (`.local/native-mcp-token-failure.log`). It verifies a failed token exchange, Pi's original visible error, absence of the old authorization banner, and successful retry in the same manager. The failure screenshot was inspected. TypeScript and formatting pass. Preview supervisor PID 7688 serves the updated source at `http://127.0.0.1:1429/`, backend port 4328; the UI returned HTTP 200. All three backend copies have SHA-256 `9F27D45914366533E9CE7E5D28CBBFADCE234642A5EC8D076AA2F19C7D871657`; executable SHA-256 is `83C419A2D2DC177F2006EAB2454903540055DECDDEC8B863016446AD7CEA4EA4`. The complete browser suite and SDK export identity audit were not rerun for this focused update.

Desktop packaging completed successfully (`.local/mcp-presentation-package.log`); installer SHA-256 is `DE82BB6A7E516C3A4FA4CCBA1456012B7AC8BC6CED9D5CE0CE1805361476B67B`.

## Previous release: identical-token MCP reauthorization

The original mapped MCP manager now completes its desktop authorization notice even when the authorization server returns identical token contents with no expiry. The credential injection hook observes a newly issued token object and reports completion only after Pi's original store successfully saves it. Metadata saves retain the loaded token object and do not prematurely complete authorization. Storage, locking, original component handlers and menu state are preserved.

This relies on the pinned Pi 1.0.0 OAuth provider preserving token object identity in metadata updates and replacing it in `saveTokens`; it requires revalidation on upgrades. It does not modify installed Pi source or introduce an extension-specific presentation adapter. The regression failed before the fix (`.local/mcp-identical-token-before.log`); all 136 integration tests now pass (`.local/mcp-identical-token-tests.log`), including a gated token response proving completion does not precede persistence. TypeScript passes. Token-exchange failures and cross-server/background authorization ownership remain unverified; full SDK compatibility is not established.

The packaged native `--mcp-oauth --mcp-identical-token` workflow passes with legacy example presentations disabled (`.local/native-mcp-identical-token.log`). It verifies unchanged stored tokens without expiry, dismissal of the authorization banner, retained manager menu, authenticated tool output, cancellation and nested Escape navigation. The native screenshot was inspected. Formatting checks pass. The owned preview was refreshed at `http://127.0.0.1:1429/` (supervisor PID 14696, backend port 4328); the UI returned HTTP 200. The complete browser suite and SDK export identity audit were not rerun for this focused update.

Desktop packaging completed successfully (`.local/mcp-identical-token-package.log`). ## Previous release: provider authorization ownership

SDK provider login now assigns one authorization ID to its URL/device-code notifications and completion. A provider completing while a newer MCP login is pending no longer emits an unscoped completion that can clear the newer banner. The desktop cancel button sends the displayed authorization ID; host cancellation targets that provider or mapped authorization only. Stale IDs are ignored. The existing no-ID cancellation API retains its global behavior.

Provider login signals include the hosted session lifetime. Cancellation, direct SDK reload and disposal close pending prompts and suppress late provider notifications. A second signal check after forwarding the original notification prevents a synchronous cancellation listener from being followed by a stale desktop URL publication. Raw SDK notification metadata, original login methods, prompt options and installation identity are retained.

Both defects were reproduced before fixes (`.local/provider-authorization-before.log`, `.local/provider-notification-before.log`). At this release all 135 integration tests passed, including concurrent provider/MCP completion, independently targeted cancellation, stale cancellation IDs and late notifications. Three desktop/narrow browser scenarios passed, including actual cancel-button request identity (`.local/provider-authorization-final-tests.log`, `.local/provider-authorization-final-browser.log`). TypeScript and formatting passed. Identical-token MCP grant completion was still open at this release and is addressed above.

The packaged `--sdk-auth` and `--mcp-oauth` native workflows pass with legacy example presentations disabled (`.local/native-provider-authorization-final.log`, `.local/native-provider-mcp-authorization.log`). ## Earlier release: authorization ownership

The desktop maps Pi component types, not individual extension identities. Hosted prompt and custom-component scopes retain ownership of authorization cancellation; mapped custom dialogs send cancellation through their original component handler. Authorization events carry IDs so completion from an older operation cannot dismiss a newer banner. Only hosted session instances are wrapped; SDK exports, prototypes and installed source remain unchanged.

Current source passes TypeScript, formatting, all 128 integration tests and the focused browser regression in `tests/ui/authorization.spec.ts`. Evidence: `.local/authorization-lifecycle-tests.log`, `.local/authorization-lifecycle-browser.log`. Three component tests now wait for the original asynchronous result callback after the dialog disappears; their exact result assertions remain intact. Direct slash-command and original mapped MCP-manager cancellation, reload and disposal close the callback listener. A deterministic immediate-cancellation regression failed for both entry points before URL publication was deferred until the caller's synchronous input setup finished; both now pass (`.local/authorization-transitions-before.log`, `.local/authorization-success-after.log`).

At this release, the public MCP credential injection hook delegated storage and locking to Pi's original implementation and ended a captured authorization when changed token values or expiry were successfully persisted. Completion was scoped by authorization identity and did not send cancellation input to the original component. The manager success regression confirmed its menu remained open after authorization completed and its callback listener closed. Identical-token grants still relied on scope closure; the update above replaces that value comparison with token-object issuance detection. Unowned authorization flows remain unverified. The SDK audit retained 458 declaration exports and 156 unchanged runtime exports (`.local/authorization-lifecycle-sdk-audit.log`).

Full SDK desktop compatibility is not established. Arbitrary render-only character drawings cannot be inferred as semantic desktop controls; see `component-mapping.md` for component coverage and upgrade constraints.

The 2026-10-04 authorization lifecycle package and original-manager native workflow pass (`.local/authorization-final-package.log`, `.local/native-authorization-final.log`). Native QA reproduced Escape closing the whole modal when replacing a control left focus on body; the generic keyboard fallback now retains original nested-menu navigation. Both viewport regressions pass, and the packaged native workflow verifies this navigation after manager sign-in. The native manager screenshot was inspected. Preview supervisor PID 20568 serves the updated source at `http://127.0.0.1:1429/` with backend port 4328.

No complete SDK compatibility claim is made.

## Requirement

Support the complete public Pi SDK functionality. Developer interfaces may be accessed in Node without individual desktop controls. Extension UI must use desktop components rather than an embedded terminal. Do not fork or patch Pi.

The compatibility unit is a Pi-provided component type, not an individual extension. Map those component instances into desktop controls and retain their original state, handlers and callbacks. Extensions composed from supported Pi components must use this shared bridge without extension-specific source matching or presentation adapters. Arbitrary character-only drawing does not supply enough semantic structure to infer desktop controls.

The objective is still active. The implementation below is verified progress, not a claim that arbitrary third-party extension interfaces have been converted.

### Latest MCP OAuth protocol and cancellation verification (2026-10-04)

A disposable loopback OAuth authorization server now exercises the installed Pi MCP implementation through the real DesktopHost: resource/authorization metadata discovery, dynamic client registration, S256 PKCE code exchange through the actual callback listener, token persistence, 401-triggered refresh with rotating refresh tokens, reload reuse, manual pasted callback, invalid-state rejection and logout. No real account or external service is involved. This proves these protocol paths with the fixture, not every authorization server or real-account interoperability.

Two desktop lifecycle gaps were reproduced. Disposing a host during MCP login let the pending action reach a cleared runtime and throw TypeError (`.local/mcp-oauth-dispose-before.log`). MCP command/status actions now check their captured session lifetime and report AbortError on retirement; retired commands do not emit `auth_complete`. Separately, `auth.cancel` did not cancel MCP login (`.local/mcp-oauth-cancel-before.log`). Login commands now propagate a combined operation/session signal through AsyncLocalStorage to their desktop input requests, and use the existing auth cancellation controller. Unrelated desktop requests retain their own lifetime. Tests verify cancellation, reload/disposal, callback listener closure and absence of stale completion events. These changes cover `mcp.command` login; login initiated inside the custom `/mcp` manager or directly as a slash prompt still needs separate cancellation verification.

All 114 integration tests passed (`.local/mcp-oauth-full-tests.log`), followed by the focused MCP-manager disposal test (`.local/mcp-manager-dispose.log`). The OAuth-focused results are `.local/mcp-oauth-lifecycle.log`; TypeScript passed. The first OAuth harness incorrectly waited for a custom component surface while `/mcp login` opens a standard input dialog; the corrected test watches `pendingDialogs`. This test correction is separate from the subsequently reproduced lifecycle defects.

The first packaged `--mcp-oauth` workflow passed token exchange through a pasted callback, a visible authenticated tool result and global cancellation. Screenshot inspection nevertheless exposed raw OSC 8 escape characters in the authorization notice. Notices now carry optional parsed presentation alongside the original message and reuse the shared desktop styled-text/link renderer. Two browser viewport cases verify visible text, color and semantic link destinations (`.local/notices-browser.log`); their screenshots were inspected. This is a generic notification presentation fix, not per-extension string rewriting.

After the notification fix, all 114 integration tests passed again (`.local/mcp-oauth-final-tests.log`). Packaged `--mcp-oauth` passed with legacy example adapters disabled (`.local/native-mcp-oauth-final.log`): the notice exposes a real link with no escape characters, the pasted callback completes token exchange, the authenticated result is visible, and `auth.cancel` dismisses the next login without issuing another token grant. The final screenshot was inspected; the raw-notice before screenshot and log are retained as `.local/screenshots/native-mcp-oauth-notice-before.png` and `.local/native-mcp-oauth-notice-before.log`. Native tests simulate authorization-server access with fetch and exercise the real desktop input/confirm controls; they do not launch an external browser in this workflow. The three backend copies match SHA-256 `4A2A28B695B8A33646E338638E56C94B001E4B14B077FCF97426EF4037E8EF41`; executable SHA-256 is `8542213E28E959B08400497D92CC0B496FE1B6A0B5269859627ECF6D87C6D168`. The preview at `http://127.0.0.1:1429/` was restarted. Full browser/native suites and SDK identity audit were not repeated; the latest identity audit is recorded in the preceding update. The complete SDK goal remains active.

Final installer packaging passed (`.local/mcp-oauth-final-package.log`), SHA-256 `208E04B8691FCBFA7F46472890578A7E75EA88F5B14B95AD632BD22C52979746`.

### Preceding MCP host-directory isolation (2026-10-04)

`DesktopHost(agentDir)` now binds MCP config, OAuth credentials, refresh locks and server logs to its own agent directory through Pi's extension options. It delegates config parsing and credential persistence to the installed Pi implementations; no Pi source or global environment is modified. The helper loads internal modules from the pinned Pi 1.0.0 package and has an explicit version guard, so an SDK upgrade requires compatibility verification. Pi's own empty-server help text still formats the process-default directory; the injected loader uses the correct host directory.

The regression first reproduced missing MCP tools when the process default directory differed from the host directory (`.local/mcp-directory-before.log`). The fixed test retains that mismatch through tool calls, resources, reconnect and reload. A second integration test creates two concurrent hosts with separate credentials and real HTTP peers, checks authenticated requests, trusted-project overrides, untrusted-project exclusion, separate server log files and one-host logout without removing the other's credentials. The unrelated process-default directory stays empty. Tokens are disposable fixture values; this does not claim a real-account OAuth flow.

All 111 integration tests, TypeScript and formatting passed for the directory change (`.local/mcp-isolation-final-tests.log`, `.local/mcp-isolation.log`). The initial full run had one external-editor test failure: it read an empty result; an unchanged isolated rerun passed. The fixture previously exposed coordination files before their writes finished. Ready/result publication now uses write-then-rename, preserving assertions and empty-result semantics. No product editor fix is claimed, and the precise scheduling of the initial failure was not captured. Its evidence remains at `.local/mcp-isolation-full-tests.log`.

Native MCP verification then exposed an unmapped Pi component used by collapsed MCP tool results: `VisualLinePreview`. The general mapper now recognizes that constructor from the installed Pi package and maps its styled text preview, preserving Pi's wrapping, start/end retention, hidden-line hint and invalidation. This is a component-type mapping, not an MCP-specific presentation. A regression captures renderer failure notices independently of tool execution success (`.local/mcp-preview-before.log`, `.local/mcp-preview-after.log`); another covers start/end content, style, width changes and updated text. All 112 integration tests passed (`.local/mcp-preview-full-tests.log`). The native test's initial option-visibility assertion also targeted a hidden HTML option; it now checks the visible selector's selected value. Initial native evidence is `.local/native-mcp-before.log`.

Packaged native `--mcp` passed with legacy example adapters disabled (`.local/native-mcp-after.log`): a real assistant tool call reaches the loopback server, its response appears in the mapped result preview, `/mcp` displays the original manager as desktop controls, Escape closes it, and no unsupported-component notice is present. ### Preceding shared component delegation and MCP verification (2026-10-04)

The bridge now recognizes transparent wrappers around Pi components without matching extension names, class names or member names. It observes direct component-valued own data properties during one synchronous render and accepts exactly one delegated child at the same width with unchanged output. Only that original child is mapped to desktop controls; unused component properties are not displayed. Temporary instance render descriptors are restored even on failure. Original wrapper input handlers, nested input ownership, replacement children and custom mouse handlers remain routed through the common bridge. This does not convert arbitrary drawing, decoration, transformed layout, private-field-only composition or frozen child instances into semantic controls. The observation can invoke wrapper render again; extension render functions must tolerate the repeated rendering already required by Pi's UI lifecycle.

A minimal regression initially failed with `Unsupported Pi TUI render-only component`; it now verifies native Input nodes, the original handler, child replacement and restored render identity. Additional tests reject changed output, repeated delegation and changed width, and verify restoration after exceptions. Pi's unmodified MCP manager opens and closes through this same mapping. A disposable loopback Streamable HTTP MCP peer verifies header authentication, discovery, real assistant-issued tool execution, resource/template listings, resource reads, reconnect and rediscovery after SDK reload. This is local protocol verification, not real remote-server OAuth or interoperability with every MCP implementation. Direct `DesktopHost(customAgentDir)` MCP configuration still follows Pi's global agent-directory resolver; these tests set the environment as desktop server startup does.

### Preceding real file-association verification (2026-10-04)

Real native file dispatch is now verified through a fresh disposable Windows file association. A handler receives the exact decoded path from mapped Text, Pi Markdown and transcript Markdown links, including Unicode, spaces, literal hash/percent/ampersand/parentheses, a target outside the workspace, and ignored URI query/fragment components. This proves the generic OS file-dispatch path; the machine's existing HTML association still has the separately recorded no-receipt problem. `--file-association` exercises the controlled association; `--file-links` still includes the existing HTML receipt assertion rather than skipping or weakening it.

Two defects were exposed and fixed. Transcript Markdown recreated its anchor renderer on every update, losing link error state; stable component identities now preserve errors across unrelated message updates. Both browser sizes reproduced the loss before the fix (`.local/file-link-state-before.log` and `.local/file-link-state-before-results`). Separately, the installed Tauri plugin's command path delegates to its detached opener without the metadata check found in a different helper; a missing file could therefore produce no error. Desktop opening now requests `files.resolveLink` with `mustExist: true`, which checks the target and reports a missing file/directory. Default path-resolution calls remain pure conversion. The actual missing-file error and successful retry are covered by native QA. This is a preflight check, not a guarantee about subsequent filesystem changes or every external application's behavior.

Superseded hosted reloads now reject with `AbortError` at the next startup-hook boundary; checks before and after the caller hook prevent obsolete extension startup. Pi's native implementation is still invoked without serialization.

## Native SDK Access

`backend/sdk-access.ts` re-exports the official SDK without a whitelist or replacements. `DesktopHost.sdk` and `withSdk(operation)` provide the actual current runtime, session, agent, session manager, settings manager, model runtime and resource loader. Callbacks, streams, constructors, event buses and custom operation implementations remain native JavaScript values.

`new DesktopHost(agentDir, { runtimeFactory })` accepts a complete runtime factory. The same factory is used for workspace changes, session replacement and imports. Installed applications also load `agentDir/desktop/runtime.mjs`:

```js
export async function createRuntime(options, createDefault, sdk) {
  return createDefault(options);
}

export function configureDesktop(context) {
  // Register desktop UI adapters here. The session is created afterwards.
}
```

Native callers can use every public SDK interface, including custom tools, providers, virtual model routers, resource loaders, session setup and replacement callbacks. Native operations are responsible for their own lifetime, session state and cleanup, as with direct SDK usage.

Desktop-hosted `session.reload()` composes the desktop cleanup with the caller's public `beforeSessionStart` hook. It retains the original session instance and delegates to its original reload implementation; SDK exports, prototypes and installed sources remain unchanged. Desktop cleanup runs before the caller hook and extension startup, retaining the editor draft while clearing old surfaces, input listeners, status/widgets, working indicators and custom completion providers. Keybindings, completion commands and themes refresh from the reloaded resources. Caller errors propagate unchanged. The instance method descriptor is restored when the session is retired; a later replacement installed by a custom runtime is not overwritten. `resources.reload` uses this same path. The runtime's session invalidation callback runs after shutdown handlers, so a theme watch enabled during shutdown is released before disposal or replacement completes.

## Private Transport

The desktop transport supplements its ordinary workflow actions with:

| Actions                                                                                         | Capability                                                                                              |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `sdk.inspect`                                                                                   | Raw session state, effective prompt, projection, entries, callable tools, routed model and cache status |
| `sdk.run`, `sdk.cancel`                                                                         | Execute a native SDK module with cooperative cancellation                                               |
| `context.refresh`, `context.edit`                                                               | Refresh the session projection and append canonical context edits                                       |
| `message.custom`, `message.user`                                                                | Native custom/user message APIs and delivery options                                                    |
| `model.cycle`, `thinking.cycle`                                                                 | Native cycling and optional persisted defaults                                                          |
| `cache.mode`, `cache.status`                                                                    | Live cache warming policy and detailed state                                                            |
| `session.bugReport`                                                                             | Generate a session bug report with cancellation                                                         |
| `bash.abort`, `bash.status`, `bash.record`                                                      | Shell cancellation, inspection and external result recording                                            |
| `compact.abort`, `summary.abort`, `retry.abort`                                                 | Independent cancellation controls                                                                       |
| `models.catalog`                                                                                | Chat, image and classifier model catalog                                                                |
| `models.request`                                                                                | Complete/stream/simple/deferred/image/classifier/router operations                                      |
| `autocomplete.suggest`, `autocomplete.apply`                                                    | Pi completion engine plus extension provider factories                                                  |
| `editor.external`, `editor.external.cancel`, `editor.external.status`, `editor.external.result` | Configured desktop editor subprocess, cancellation, status and recoverable completed text               |
| `desktop.action`, `desktop.close`                                                               | Native extension component interactions and cancellation                                                |
| `desktop.mouse`                                                                                 | Original Pi pointer dispatch, capture target, delegated focus and render results                        |
| `desktop.overlay.measure`                                                                       | Native overlay height feedback with instance/layout identity validation                                 |
| `desktop.input`, `desktop.key`, `desktop.focus`, `desktop.viewport`                             | Native input hooks, shortcuts, component keys, focus and overlay geometry                               |

All original `AgentSessionEvent` values are forwarded as `sdk_event`. Snapshots contain a backend instance ID and a monotonically increasing revision so delayed action responses cannot overwrite newer event state. Direct model streams emit `activity` events named `sdk:model:<operation-id>` and return the final response. These requests do not append conversation messages implicitly.

SDK authentication receives `SettingsManager.getOrCreateDeviceId()` through `LoginOptions.getDeviceId`, including the unmodified ChatGPT OAuth flow. Login prompts preserve selection IDs separately from labels/descriptions, input placeholders and secret fields. Prompt abort signals combine provider and desktop cancellation, including cancellation during dialog publication. Original auth events are forwarded as `activity` events named `auth`, preserving link and device-code timing metadata. Closing a prompt remounts its modal by dialog ID to prevent an empty modal after rapid cancellation.

`sdk.run` loads `.mjs`/`.js` operation modules from `agentDir/desktop` or a trusted project's `.pi/desktop` directory, checking real paths. Its default export receives `(context, args)`. All SDK calls happen in Node; returned transport data must be JSON serializable.

Void operation results are `null` over the private transport. Error responses contain strings, including empty strings and non-Error rejections; desktop `action()` rejects with an `Error`. Direct `withSdk()` retains original results and rejection identity. Functions/symbols, BigInt and cyclic results cannot cross JSON transports; callbacks and live objects remain available in the Node context.

```js
export default async ({ session, signal, emit, sdk }, args) => {
  const unsubscribe = session.subscribe((event) => emit("session", event));
  try {
    return await session.summarizeForBugReport({ hint: args.hint, signal });
  } finally {
    unsubscribe();
  }
};
```

## Desktop Extension Components

The default host maps all 17 standard pi-tui component types plus Pi's `CustomEditor`, `BorderedLoader`, `DynamicBorder` and `VisualLinePreview` into desktop controls while running the original extension factories and renderers. No name, source hash or extension-specific adapter is required for standard components and their composition. See [component mapping and compatibility limits](component-mapping.md).

`context.desktop.registerAdapter({ id, matches, create })` remains available for explicitly supplied native presentations. These controllers take precedence over the generic component bridge and supply `view()`, `handleAction()`, optional `dispose()`, and optional editor text APIs. The node schema is `shared/desktop-ui.ts`.

Adapters receive the original source factory, the destination slot, the native SDK context, a lifecycle `signal`, `done(value)` and `invalidate()`. `done()` resolves `ctx.ui.custom()` with the original SDK callback semantics. Closing or aborting a dialog resolves it with `undefined`. Session replacement tears down mounted controllers before the SDK invalidates the old extension context. Asynchronous adapter construction is supported; callbacks from replaced controllers cannot close their successors.

Supported destinations are dialogs, header/footer, above/below the editor, custom editors, messages, custom entries and tool call/result rows. Renderer adapters receive a `DesktopRenderSource` with the original renderer, raw data, partial/error/expansion context and shared per-tool state. Controllers may implement `update(source)` to retain their state across streaming and expansion changes.

Custom dialogs support positioned overlays with dynamic sizing/visibility and `hide`, `setHidden`, `focus`, `unfocus`, `isFocused`, `isHidden` and `getBounds` handles. Adapters can expose `focusTarget` to map an original component passed to `unfocus({ target })`. Native editor controllers must implement `getText` and `setText`; text is synchronized on mounting and restoring the default editor.

The generic mapper also connects direct `tui.showOverlay()` / `hideOverlay()` calls to child desktop surfaces. Pi retains its original overlay stack, including explicit focus targets and stacked focus restoration. Child component callbacks remain available while an asynchronous parent factory awaits input. Parent cancellation closes its child surfaces and releases their mapped resources. This uses component types rather than extension-specific presentation adapters.

Overlay geometry calls Pi's original layout resolver with the logical desktop viewport. Original components render at the resolved width; DOM height feedback then positions native controls using their visible height. Instance/layout guards reject stale measurements, and a reentrancy guard supports bounds reads inside original renders. Recursive width propagation retains Box/ScrollView constraints and Pi's stack allocation/visibility semantics without modifying SDK prototypes or source.

Pointer state is independent per `pointerId` in both the frontend and backend. Delayed rejected captures, simultaneous captured touches, surviving outside drags and multi-touch selection guards on a native input have CDP browser/native workflow coverage. Physical touch devices and mobile keyboards remain a separate validation boundary.

The original factory's `tui.terminal.setTitle()` updates the document and native desktop window; `setProgress()` maps to the native indeterminate progress indicator. Direct TUI progress calls share one generation-wide last-writer state. Closing or failing a factory retains its title/progress handles and active progress until an explicit write or generation teardown. Independent opaque/PTY OSC effect owners retain their own lifetimes. Resource/session replacement retires the direct state and prevents old-generation callbacks from changing its successor. Snapshots retain the current title/progress for reconnecting clients. Repeated title writes during original renders do not generate a refresh loop. Native commands run in order with explicit Tauri permissions.

Native key/paste input reaches `onTerminalInput` listeners in order, with consume/replace semantics, Pi's public key parser and extension shortcut callbacks. Converted components can implement `handleKey(event)` or `handleInput(data, event, context)` for transformed raw input. Editor controllers may expose selection, paste and `addToHistory` APIs. Consumed command replies can return an `editor` text/selection transaction, applied before the next queued key. Native textarea selection events synchronize the SDK caret; server selection revisions and associated text prevent stale caret resets. Each mount has an `instanceId`; React remounts its controls and obsolete input/actions are ignored. Browser completion supports configured keyboard selection, explicit completion and caret changes. Theme colors use Pi's public color conversion and are available as native CSS tokens.

Theme APIs include Pi's reserved `system` theme and both built-in file themes, including custom runtimes using `noThemes`. Initial binding and session replacement apply the saved setting, defaulting to `system`. Named changes persist settings; direct `Theme` instances preserve the saved name. Changes update the original SDK theme helpers and desktop CSS tokens together. Resource reload reapplies changed theme files before extension startup. Missing named themes return failure and fall back to system without overwriting the saved setting. The desktop reports system appearance before initialization and on media-query changes; automatic theme pairs and named system themes follow it, while fixed names and in-memory instances retain their selection. The settings appearance selector calls the same SDK API and preserves its system selection across changes.

Active named custom themes watch their actual source path, including project/resource themes with a different filename. Directory watching retains updates after atomic replacement. Pi's original JSON validator and loader preserve the last valid theme during syntax/schema errors or a temporarily missing file. Watches and pending reloads are released on selection changes, resource reload, session replacement and disposal. Settings, trust, package and configuration actions share the desktop reload lifecycle. Project trust regressions verify project theme/settings activation and removal. The system theme uses Pi's fallback colors for the reported appearance, without querying a terminal palette.

Arbitrary render-only components use the generic xterm.js path. Observable standard-component descendants map to desktop controls when their ownership and geometry can be identified; original custom drawing is retained through terminal frames. Standard-component subclasses retain original input logic and the documented additions/frame mapping. Inaccessible ownership and ambiguous geometry remain limits on semantic desktop conversion.

Standard Text/TruncatedText source colors and compound decorations map to structured desktop spans through an SGR state normalizer, Anser and Pi's palette conversion. OSC 8 HTTP/HTTPS links use Pi's parser and the existing desktop link handler. Text/Box background callbacks and Markdown default backgrounds map to native content/padding areas. Markdown uses its original instance's token tree and theme renderers for native semantic blocks, code highlighting, default-style resets, inline ANSI, source options and LaTeX. Control captions, prompt/placeholder transformations, editor/border colors, selection/setting themes, descriptions and loader styling also use that shared bridge. Status/working messages and string widgets share text runs and links through `extensionUI.textPresentation`; their raw strings remain available to SDK callers and footer data providers. Original text updates and submit callbacks retain their behavior. Terminal control instructions remain inert and literal HTML stays text. Mixed option/placeholder styles use structured desktop controls. Repeated style enables reset once; colon RGB/palette colors, underline variants and independent underline colors/overline are supported. Further SGR variants and non-HTTP links remain separate integration work.

### Legacy Example Conversions

`backend/official-extensions.ts`, `backend/official-workflows.ts` and `backend/official-editor.ts` retain six older example presentations. They are disabled by default and enabled only through `legacyExampleAdapters: true` or `PI_DESKTOP_LEGACY_EXAMPLE_ADAPTERS=1`. This opt-in loader override supports legacy regression tests; it is separate from the component mapping architecture.

| Extension             | Native presentation                   | Preserved behavior                                                                                                                                            |
| --------------------- | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `question.ts`         | Selection and custom answer dialog    | Original tool validation, results, cancellation and tool abort                                                                                                |
| `questionnaire.ts`    | Tabs, free text and review table      | Option values, partial answers, original results and cancellation                                                                                             |
| `todo.ts`             | Todo table and tool output            | Original tool execution and branch reconstruction; detached result snapshots prevent mutation of historical state                                             |
| `qna.ts`              | Extraction progress and Cancel        | Original factory starts the actual model request; completion fills the editor; Cancel and global Abort cancel that request                                    |
| `message-renderer.ts` | Status message and expanded timestamp | Original custom message metadata and Pi session persistence                                                                                                   |
| `modal-editor.ts`     | Native textarea and mode indicator    | Modes, navigation, SDK selection/paste, completion, submission and common control actions; see [editor coverage and remaining differences](editor-support.md) |

Only the opt-in legacy path recognizes audited source hashes. Modified or unrelated factories use the standard component bridge when their component structure is supported.

The original question tools' `execute()` runs with a context that replaces only `ui.custom()`. The native descriptor contains the actual tool parameters, original factory and extension path. This preserves the original validation and result formatting. For the question and Todo dialogs, the skipped factories contain presentation logic only. The Q&A factory also starts a model request, so its original factory executes against an unstarted `TuiMainScreen` whose terminal interface rejects output. Its loader provides cancellation and cleanup; React supplies the displayed progress control. No terminal output is rendered. A global SDK theme is initialized only if one is missing, without a terminal watcher.

Native controls support option descriptions, custom answers, questionnaire defaults, option values separate from labels, tabs, review before submission and disabled submission until all answers are saved. Closing a questionnaire retains saved partial answers and returns `cancelled: true`; a single-question cancellation returns `null`. Tool abort signals close their own dialogs and release listeners. Transcript rendering uses native text and tables.

Custom runtime factories that replace the resource loader must connect this override to their own loader if they want these built-in conversions. The full native SDK API remains available to do so.

Pi restores the last appended session entry when reopening a file. Navigating without appending does not persist a preferred leaf. A fresh session containing only custom messages stays in memory until a user or assistant message is added. The desktop preserves these SDK rules.

## Verification And Remaining Work

Current SDK 1.0.0 regression suite contains 96 integration tests and 69 browser scenarios. The latest link update passed all 96 integration tests and eight focused browser text/Markdown/link scenarios. The preceding editor-configuration release completed the remaining browser coverage, including three pointer tests rerun after a test-coordinate correction. TypeScript checks pass. The declaration audit inventories 458 exports and verifies identity of all 156 native runtime exports. Packaged Tauri WebView2 verification uses bundled Node and covers the generic component bridge, direct nested overlays, pointer capture/focus, horizontal native controls, shared-component clicks, clipped scroll hits and clean Ctrl+D exit. The focused `--editor-navigation` workflow compares default, custom and dialog editor movement to native textarea references, including resize while an earlier input acknowledgement remains pending. `--sdk-themes` checks theme helpers, persistence, fallback, media-query appearance changes, active-file updates and actual desktop colors. `--input-replacement` exercises multiline native text insertion, original handlers/listeners, range undo and CDP composition, and is included in both mapped-component and full native QA. It also covers the single-line Input's original undo and submission callbacks, and selected Backspace/Delete reaching original extension handlers with consume semantics. The text-style workflows check actual DOM styles, backgrounds, web-link clicks, first-line truncation, inert terminal instructions, literal HTML and original component callbacks. Status/string-widget cases also verify raw SDK strings, styling across lines, working text, placement changes, component/string replacement and cleanup. Markdown workflows additionally check original theme functions, semantic blocks, code highlighting, inline ANSI, source options and default-style resets. Control workflows verify actual prompt/placeholder styles, native selection labels with original values, unselected option color reset, filtering/descriptions, borders and loader updates/cancellation. Mixed-control workflows additionally exercise portalled menus, original input listeners and callbacks, rich placeholders and links, source text resets, and long-list selection scrolling/focus.

Browser regression cases deliberately reorder action snapshots and editor acknowledgements, settle selection replies during rapid input after changing focus, and delay Pi pointer decisions during dragging and newer focus changes. Native form pointer tests also exercise parent regions/containers, settings callbacks and explicit capture outside the control; a separate native overlay check repeats focus restoration five times. Layout regressions cover horizontal/shared occurrences, clipped scrolling, gaps, synchronous render inside mouse callbacks, handler replacement and exception cleanup. SDK editor transactions commit the controlled React value before the next queued key reads it.

Mapped-editor regressions cover expanded large-paste reads, text synchronization, editor restoration, selection typing/paste/deletion, atomic original undo, synchronous change callbacks and callback/error cleanup, plus expanded prompt submission/history. Paste previews preserve full content, copy/remove/undo and stale-command rejection. Registered paste markers retain atomic caret/selection boundaries while literal marker-looking text stays editable. Synthetic IME commits preserve the original selected range, cancellation, subclass transformations and extension input listener consume/replace. Both final-input event orders and consecutive commits are exercised, including an older acknowledgement arriving during a newer composition. Pi's original undo coalescing remains active. These workflows pass at both browser widths and in the packaged native WebView2 application.

Completion confirmations belong to the editor and follow queued keyboard input, retaining their command identity when Pi replaces a suggestion list. If an earlier Tab already completed the text, a delayed confirmation is ignored. Delayed browser tests and the focused native `--mapped-completion` reproduction verify the absence of unavailable-action notices. This verifies the documented component contract, not complete parity with arbitrary terminal UI implementations.

Ordinary typing and paste immediately after a composition commit now read acknowledged text/selection inside the event queue. Repeated text is preserved while duplicate final-input events are suppressed using the DOM value. Browser regressions exercise immediate input, repeated text, paste and both final-input orders. Chromium CDP-generated composition/input cases cover cancellation, transformations and undo; CDP emits an untrusted composition-end event. Real OS IME remains outside this validation.

One native full-workflow run submitted the previous draft immediately after replacing extension-supplied text. A reduced native scenario passed five repetitions, then five more with the preceding model/tool round; a browser scenario with pending draft synchronization and the subsequent full native run also passed. No product fix is claimed for this intermittent observation. `npx tsx tests/native.ts --draft-submit` retains the focused request-payload check for further reproduction.

During the 2026-10-04 component deletion verification, one browser suite run encountered `Failed to fetch` during editor navigation. The focused navigation rerun and following complete 38-scenario run passed. One native input-replacement run timed out before SDK initialization while the browser suite was also running; its isolated rerun passed. No startup or transport fix is claimed for these intermittent observations.

The focused `--editor-navigation` workflow passed in an earlier release verification.

The 2026-10-04 insertion-order repair reproduces a concrete race adjacent to the preceding native drag-selection failure: after a drag, two immediate replacements could execute with the first request's resulting end caret, appending the second replacement and losing the browser range. The two-iteration native loop failed twice with `Previous text 1Native input selection`; a 100 ms delayed-mouse browser loop reproduced the same result. DOM event/transaction traces are retained in `.local/native-pointer-trace.log`. Mapped insertions now capture their original range, display provisional text without a duplicate edit, rebase through preceding handler results and preserve a later browser selection. The default desktop controller retains its native undo path. The original isolated one-off drag failure is not independently proven to share this exact cause.

Verified with the real SDK and local fixtures:

- Identity of every public SDK runtime export and native event-bus callbacks.
- Reapplying `ui.theme` unwraps the host's live proxy to its actual theme instance, including proxies retained across session replacement. SDK color helpers, desktop tokens and saved named settings remain consistent.
- Component terminal title/progress, independent progress ownership, failed-factory cleanup, cancellation, session replacement, stale callbacks and repeated titles during render. Browser cases exercise both widths; native QA reads the actual window title and observes successful progress commands, without inspecting OS taskbar pixels.
- Authentication installation identity, duplicate selection labels with distinct IDs, descriptions, input placeholders, secret prompts, original auth metadata, credential persistence/logout and cancellation during prompt publication.
- Unmodified ChatGPT authorization initialization and cancellation before token exchange, without a real account or network token request.
- Custom runtime factories, resource loaders and tool definitions across session replacement.
- Session initialization callbacks and live SDK references across replacement.
- Native SDK module loading, project trust checks and cancellation.
- Custom message visibility, canonical context edits and virtual routing.
- Direct streaming, image generation, classification, deferred fetch/cancel and catalogs.
- Custom shell backends, desktop abort, external result recording and completion factories.
- Native extension form actions, disabled controls, return values, cancellation and disposal.
- Native input listener consume/replace/unsubscribe, shortcuts, virtual input/composition events and session teardown.
- Native tool/message/entry renderer slots, expansion updates and disposal.
- Overlay dynamic bounds, hide/show/focus, responsive positioning and editor text restoration.
- Original Pi overlay sizing/anchors/offsets/margins/percentages, resolved render width, desktop height feedback, stale/reentrant measurement guards and recursive padding/stack visibility widths. Browser workflows resize between desktop/narrow widths; native workflows compare actual DOM geometry to published bounds.
- Pi pointer dispatch and specific child capture targets, delegated container/overlay focus, implicit handled-press retention, original click counts, cancellation, retired component cleanup and explicit render flags.
- Independent simultaneous touch capture/rejection, delayed press replies, releasing one pointer before the surviving outside drag, and multi-pointer native-input selection guards through Chromium CDP in browser and packaged WebView2 workflows. Physical touch hardware is not included.
- Original mouse handlers on native Input/Editor/CustomEditor/SelectList subclasses; ancestor bubbling, padded container routing, settings cycling/submenus and keyboard activation, explicit native capture outside bounds, browser drag selection, single callbacks and text transactions with `render: false`.
- DOM component hit paths distinguish shared horizontal occurrences and clipped scroll children. Instance-local dispatch preserves original handlers and layout writes across synchronous refresh and errors; vertical gaps fall back once without invoking unrelated children.
- Delayed selection from an older overlay cannot reclaim focus from a newer overlay; verified in native WebView2 and component integration tests.
- Desktop and narrow-screen extension form interactions and screenshots.
- Native Tauri WebView2, bundled Node, real SDK tools/inspection, converted extension forms, input hooks/shortcuts, transcript renderers and overlays.
- Unmodified official question/questionnaire extensions through actual model tool calls: captured parameters, original results, reload/session replacement, custom answers, partial cancellation and abort. Modified-source rejection is also verified.
- Official Todo branch reconstruction, immutable historical state, reload, session replacement, clear and reset.
- Official Q&A work initiated inside its original factory, editor completion, Cancel and global Abort, including cancellation observed by the HTTP test server.
- Official status message metadata, expanded timestamps and session restoration.
- All six native presentations in browser workflows at desktop/narrow viewports and in packaged native WebView2. Modal editor workflows cover modes, selection/paste, completion, undo, synthetic composition, submission, clear, abort and default restoration. Empty-editor Ctrl+D closes the native window cleanly.

The declaration inventory records native API availability separately from desktop behavior. All 28 `ExtensionUIContext` members have host implementations; the component bridge and the behavioral limits below remain separate requirements. Validation commands are `npm run check`, `npm test`, `npm run test:ui`, `npm run test:native`, `npm run audit:sdk` and `npm run desktop:build`.

Still required before claiming full support:

- Complete and validate the [component bridge's remaining behavior](component-mapping.md), particularly terminal-only decorations, arbitrary custom container geometry and additional physical touch/browser gesture behavior. The official overlay resolver, original render width, native height feedback and independent pointer states now have focused coverage. Native form subclasses, ancestor mouse regions/containers, horizontal/shared occurrences, clipped scroll children and settings handlers share the generic mouse bridge.
- Validate additional editor variants, selection/undo combinations and bidi caret affinity. Browser-measured visual-row navigation covers default, custom and dialog editors, proportional fonts, Unicode, repeated movement, paging, Shift selection and resize during pending navigation acknowledgements. Paste previews, atomic marker boundaries, copy/remove/undo and stale controls have integration/browser/native coverage. Synthetic IME coverage includes original handlers/listeners, cancellation, final-input event ordering and consecutive commits without overwriting an active newer composition. Standard-component mapping does not establish parity with arbitrary render-only components.
- Validate live IME/mobile keyboard behavior, unusual terminal input transformations and complex editor/overlay workflows against those extensions.

The [API-by-API audit](sdk-api-audit.md) and generated declaration inventory record native availability separately from desktop conversion and behavioral verification. Run `npm run audit:sdk` when upgrading the pinned SDK.

Real-account OAuth/token exchange, paid providers, remote MCP servers and all third-party packages are not covered by the local fixtures.

The preceding 2026-10-04 stack-size update passed TypeScript, formatting, all 91 integration tests, all 65 browser scenarios, the SDK identity audit and desktop packaging. Zero-size HStack/VStack entries preserve gaps and original instances while hiding desktop controls; original min/max constraints and stale-action rejection have regression coverage. Packaged `--stack-sizes` passed with legacy presentations disabled, and desktop/narrow plus native screenshots were inspected. The first packaged `--mapped-components` run timed out waiting for expanded paste submission to appear in session messages at `tests/component-workflows.ts:771`; its screenshot is retained at `.local/screenshots/native-stack-editor-timeout.png`. An unchanged rerun passed (`.local/native-stack-mapped-rerun.log`). The cause of this isolated timeout has not been established, and no editor fix is claimed. The preceding 2026-10-04 hidden-interaction update separates retained component lifetime from currently mapped interaction targets. Hidden children now lose keyboard focus, mouse capture and stale hit occurrences while retaining original instances. Regressions verify restored input and exactly-once disposal, plus cancellation of a captured drag when its child collapses. Native QA passed on its first run for this update (`.local/native-hidden-interaction.log`). The full 65-browser run and SDK identity audit were last run in the preceding stack-size update. This does not diagnose or claim to fix that update's isolated paste-submission timeout. All three backend copies have SHA-256 `F13B6D7ED8715511AD8614EB8A2EE335215BFFBDB9090004382216DB972E2047`.

Their helper now measures actual padding/line height to click the content start, without changing the original insertion assertion. All three and both editor-configuration cases passed in a five-case rerun. Initial failure traces are retained under `.local/editor-config-fixed-coordinate-evidence`. Packaged `--mapped-components` and `--editor-config` passed, with legacy presentations disabled; logs are `.local/native-editor-config-mapped.log` and `.local/native-editor-config.log`. The latest 2026-10-04 link update supports mailto/tel links alongside HTTP/HTTPS in Pi Text OSC 8 output and Markdown metadata, using shared backend/client URL validation and normalization. The packaged opener now has both its command permission and default URL scope. Before the fix, real native HTTP opening failed with `Not allowed to open url` (`.local/native-link-before.log`). After rebuilding, the real native opener launched a disposable local HTTP page in the default browser, and the local server observed the request (`.local/native-link-after-launch.log`, `.local/native-links-after.log`). Email/phone click destinations are checked using a test-only interceptor at Tauri's fetch transport, without dispatching those calls to OS handlers during the final verification. An initial attempt to replace the read-only internal invoke function did not install; the final interceptor verifies installation and restores the original transport. Native mail/phone handler availability is not established. TypeScript, formatting, all 96 integration tests, eight focused browser cases, packaged `--links` and `--link-launch`, and desktop packaging passed. Desktop/narrow and native screenshots were inspected. The full mapped-component native workflow was last run in the preceding editor-configuration update; file/custom-protocol links remain open work. All three backend copies have SHA-256 `E2D28616ED0EF14ADCAED485326CCCECFC6C6B38924F5401D2ED65932B9D6654`; the executable has `8B80B4186F14E3B6B2AFDE13532F1C633F5FA64CCEE8199B50A9CDE6175A9A1E`; the installer has `40F88664A712D3CF5821C2EF4522061E8235D3D62B0818046535A23925280CB4`.

## Original renderer modes, redraw and lifecycle

Original Pi regular/fullscreen renderer replacement, redraw scheduling/accounting and terminal lifecycle now execute through the desktop drawing backend. SDK settings initialize the renderer, including Pi 1.0.0's unconfigured fullscreen default. The stable TUI reference, terminal, application roots/editor/footer/provider and mapping instances survive replacement. Native overlay entries block a different mode even when hidden. Context input subscriptions rebind; direct hooks remain on the old renderer, matching Pi. Fullscreen viewport input runs once before extensions. Raw extension terminal calls retain the physical xterm.js/PTY path, while internal application redraws do not open a duplicate terminal UI. See [the renderer contract](renderer-modes.md).

The shared runtime now uses Pi's original createInteractiveTui/createChatViewport factories, regular render-state capture/restore and stable reference proxy. Force redraws, coalesced requests, counters, native frame state, fullscreen follow/scroll/search APIs, resize and start/stop options execute the original renderer. A synchronous drawing lease routes internal escape sequences to desktop presentation; direct extension calls and async callbacks retain physical terminal ownership. A source-only host without a PTY continues to reject unsupported raw operations. Private surface layout TUIs remain detached. The layout/sink does not emulate arbitrary device replies or establish exact desktop/private terminal geometry.

Initialization honors settings and the SDK's fullscreen default. Mode replacement preserves original components and terminal identity, rejects all native overlay entries, rebinds context terminal input and retargets stored TUI methods. Direct listener Sets/unsubscribe closures have the original per-renderer behavior. The fullscreen constructor's viewport hook runs before desktop/global hooks once. Transcript/resume-hint exit policies use original lifecycle hooks; retirement still frees all ownership if an extension invalidate/render hook throws.

Whole-category implementation preceded grouped verification and consolidated repairs. Initial source coverage reports **1067/1076**, with nine failures, in .local/renderer-modes-source-group1.log. One verified themes.test.ts worker had completed its tests but retained resources after transcript exit threw from an extension invalidate hook; only that owned worker was stopped, with identity recorded in .local/renderer-modes-source-group1-stuck-worker.json. The final full source group uses four workers and passes **1078/1078** (.local/renderer-modes-source-repair.log), including an explicit SDK-default-fullscreen case and a throwing-fullscreen-exit cleanup regression. Old regular-mode component comparisons now set regular explicitly in the fixture; product initialization continues to use SDK settings. A resize test waits for the actual native row count instead of assuming a 45ms scheduled frame. Test type declarations were corrected.

The initial browser group passes **55/59** (.local/renderer-modes-ui-group1.log). The new tests initially assumed a switch away from regular while Pi's default is fullscreen; returning regular can also restore an identical screen with zero full redraws, so force accounting is now tested explicitly. The second group passes **35/59** (.local/renderer-modes-ui-repair.log): the new mode workflow resumed TUI input while its intentionally opened terminal panel still covered the clicked control, causing a timeout; three later new cases saw the retained fixture, and 20 continuity cases saw its extra registration. The helper collapses the terminal using its existing control and guarantees fixture cleanup. The final full affected group passes **59/59** (.local/renderer-modes-ui-final.log), including four new wide/narrow mapped-input/xterm mode workflows. Product code is unchanged by the browser-helper repairs.

One final type-checked unbundled build passes (.local/renderer-modes-native-build.log), with all three backend artifacts updated. Six native groups pass in the initial sequential batch: shared-tui, runtime-continuity, default-editor, terminal-state, overlay-lifecycle and terminal. Renderer-modes reaches its default 5-second editor assertion before any QA action is recorded; its helper now waits up to 30 seconds for the actual editor. That group passes both mapped-input/xterm workflows against the unchanged executable (.local/renderer-modes-native-repair.log). Its four browser overlaps pass (.local/renderer-modes-ui-overlap.log); the overlap does not increase the 59 distinct browser scenarios. No product startup correction or definitive delay cause is claimed. There was no native product repair or rebuild. Final types and formatting pass, and the SDK audit retains 458 declaration exports, 156 unchanged runtime exports, 38 constructors and 16 declaration statics (15 runtime).

The existing 1451 preview backend was refreshed after checking its recorded process identity and idle state; Vite was reused. Initialization/snapshot are verified and no browser tab was added. The original tab connection remains unverified. Representative wide/narrow/native screenshots were inspected. .local/renderer-modes-receipt.json records the evidence and boundaries. This category does not establish full SDK completion; the broader application/notice/protocol/private-geometry and external/platform/version limits above remain open.
